This local uniqueness implies that the number of solutions for

\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=0
equals that of
{\bf{K}}_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=0
.

7.1.
The improved expansion for
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)

We will follow the strategies mentioned above. The most technical part is the expansion of

\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
.

For any type III critical point
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
with
\Omega_{\varepsilon}=\Omega\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
, it holds
|x_{\varepsilon}|,|y_{\varepsilon}|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
with
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
and
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
.
Then we have following results.

Lemma 7.1
.

For
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
and
j=1,2
, if
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, then it holds

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left[\big(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}\big)\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right]+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2-3\beta}\Big),\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left[\big(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}\big)\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}(y_{j}-x_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right]+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2-3\beta}\Big).\end{cases}

(7.2)

Proof.

First, by (
2.3
), we recall

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=&-\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left[\frac{\Lambda_{1}x_{j}}{|x|^{2}-\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}(|y|^{2}x_{j}-\varepsilon^{2}y_{j})}{|x|^{2}|y|^{2}-2\varepsilon^{2}x\cdot y+\varepsilon^{4}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right].\end{split}

Also from
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, we see

\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}-\varepsilon^{2}}=\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{(|x|^{2}-\varepsilon^{2})|x|}\right)=\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2-3\beta}\Big),

and

\frac{|y|^{2}x_{j}-\varepsilon^{2}y_{j}}{|x|^{2}|y|^{2}-2\varepsilon^{2}x\cdot y+\varepsilon^{4}}=\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}|x|\cdot|y|+\varepsilon^{4}}{(|x|^{2}|y|^{2}-2\varepsilon^{2}x\cdot y+\varepsilon^{4})|x|}\right)=\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2-3\beta}\Big).

Hence from above computations, we get the first estimate of (
7.2
). Similarly, the second estimate of (
7.2
) holds.
∎

We now expand
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
until
the effect from the location of the small hole can be seen.

Proposition 7.2
.

For
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
and
j=1,2
, if
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, it holds

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left\{\frac{h(x,y)x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right\}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left\{\frac{h(x,y)y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}+\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}(y_{j}-x_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right\}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{cases}

(7.3)

where

h(x,y):=\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}.

(7.4)

Proof.

First, from (
7.2
) and (
A.12
), we have

\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left[\big(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}\big)\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right]+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

(7.5)

where
\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)
is the function in (
A.13
).
Now we compute the term
\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)
. We have

\begin{split}\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=-\frac{\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|}{2\pi}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|}{2\pi}+(\Lambda_{1}H_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}H_{\Omega}(0,y))}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=-\frac{{h}(x,y)}{2\pi}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{split}

and then

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+2\pi\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=-\frac{{h}(x,y)x_{j}}{2\pi|x|^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Also, by Taylor’s expansion, we have

\begin{split}&\Lambda_{1}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)}{\partial x_{j}}+2\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big).\end{split}

Hence from above computations, we get

\begin{split}\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=&\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left\{-\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}\big({h}(x,y)-\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\big)-\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right\}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.6)

Finally, from (
7.5
) and (
7.6
), we prove the first estimate of (
7.3
). Similarly, it is possible to deduce the second estimate of (
7.3
).
∎

Remark 7.3
.

Let
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
be a type III critical point of

\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
. Set

(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon}):=\big(\frac{x_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}},\frac{x_{\varepsilon}+\tau y_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}\big)
. Then
from (
6.10
), we have

\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\frac{x_{\varepsilon}+\tau y_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}=\frac{w_{\varepsilon}+\tau z_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}=O\big(1\big).

Hence we get that the type III critical points of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
must belong to
\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}
, where

\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}:=\left\{(x,y)\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\times\Omega_{\varepsilon},~|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta},~~\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big|\frac{x+\tau y}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}\Big|<\infty~~~\mbox{with}~~~\beta=\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})^{2}}~~~\mbox{and}~~~\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}\right\}.

To determine the direction of the critical points, we introduce following transform

(w,\gamma)=\big(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{\beta}},\frac{x+\tau y}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}\big),~~\mbox{with}~~\beta=\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{2}}~~~\mbox{and}~~~\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}.

We rewrite the expansion of
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)

as follows.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}:=\Big\{(w,\gamma);~(x,y):=\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\big)\in\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}\Big\}
, then for any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
, it holds

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\big)}\\
=&-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{k\big(|w|,\tau\big)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}\right\}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

(7.7)

and

\begin{split}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\big)}\\
=&-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[-\frac{\tau k\big(|w|,\tau\big)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}\right\}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

(7.8)

where

k(r,\tau):=(1+\tau)\big(\ln r+2(1-\beta)\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)-\ln\tau.

(7.9)

Proof.

The first estimate of (
7.3
) gives

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=&-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(1+\tau)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}-\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{\tau+1}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right\}\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{\pi|x-y|^{2}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

(7.10)

Also letting
(x,y)=\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\big)
, by Taylor’s expansion, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta\ln\varepsilon+\ln|w|)-\ln\tau}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

(7.11)

\begin{split}\frac{x_{j}-y_{j}}{|x-y|^{2}}=&\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}\left[(1+\tau)w_{j}+\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big(\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\gamma_{j}\Big)\right]+O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big).\end{split}

(7.12)

Hence inserting (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) into (
7.10
), we deduce (
7.7
).

Similarly, from the second estimate of (
7.3
), we get

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=&-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(1+\tau)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}-\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{\tau+1}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right\}\\
&\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}(y_{j}-x_{j})}{\pi|x-y|^{2}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Also, by Taylor’s expansion, we know

\begin{split}\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}=&-\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}\left[w_{j}+\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big(\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\gamma_{j}\Big)\right]+O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big).\end{split}

(7.13)

So from (
7.11
) and (
7.13
), we deduce

\begin{split}&\left[\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(1+\tau)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}\\
=&-\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}\left[\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta\ln\varepsilon+\ln|w|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))-\ln\tau}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\frac{\tau^{2}}{(1+\tau)|w|^{2}}\Big[\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\gamma_{j}\Big]+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Hence (
7.8
) follows by above computations.
∎

Now we define
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)
on
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
as follows:

{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\Big(\nabla_{x}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y),\nabla_{y}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)\Big)\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}.

(7.14)

Also

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)
is given by

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\Big(\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\Big),

with

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma)=-\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w+\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)-\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma,\\[11.38109pt]
\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)=\left[\frac{\pi k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w+\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)-\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma,\end{cases}

and
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
,
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
,
k(r,\tau)
is the function in (
7.9
). Then Proposition
7.4
means that

{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}&{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}\\[8.53581pt]
{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}&\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)~~~\mbox{for any}~~~(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},

(7.15)

where
{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\[5.69054pt]
0&1\\
\end{array}\right)
and
{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\[5.69054pt]
0&0\\
\end{array}\right)
. Furthermore we give a
C^{1}
-estimate of (
7.7
) and (
7.8
).

Proposition 7.5
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
,
it holds

\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}&{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}\\[8.53581pt]
{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}&\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).

Proof.

First, we denote by

{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big({\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,m}(w,\gamma)=\big({{V}}_{\varepsilon,m,1}(w,\gamma),{{V}}_{\varepsilon,m,2}(w,\gamma)\big)\,\,\mbox{with}\,\,m=1,2.

Next we have by Lemma
A.6
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{{V}}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\varepsilon^{\beta}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\beta}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{i}}+\frac{\partial\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}}\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}\partial y_{i}}+\frac{\partial\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}}\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left\{\Big[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\Big]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\varepsilon^{1-2\beta}}{\tau}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}\partial y_{i}}+\frac{\partial\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}}\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\left\{\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

where
\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)
is the function in (
A.13
).
Hence for any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
, it holds

\begin{split}\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{V}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)=&\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\left\{\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.16)

From (
2.3
) and (
A.13
), for any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
we can compute directly that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left[\Big(\frac{(1+\tau)(1-\beta)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}-\frac{2\beta(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\Big)w_{j}-\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]=\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\beta}{\pi}\left[\delta_{ij}-\frac{2w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}}\frac{\partial}{\partial w_{i}}\left[\frac{((1+\tau)\ln|w|+(\beta-1)\ln\varepsilon)w_{j}}{(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))|w|^{2}}\right]+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Thus from above computations, we deduce that for any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma)=\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Similarly, for any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)=\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Hence we complete the proofs of Proposition
7.5
.
∎

7.2.
The case
\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
(Proof of Theorem
1.16
)

In the case
\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
, the expansion in
(
7.15
) is sufficient.

From (
7.15
) and Proposition
7.5
, it is essential to consider the solution of
\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
.
We write

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
as for
j=1,2
,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\beta\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\tau^{2}\beta\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}\\[8.53581pt]
\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}\\
\end{array}\right).

We denote the matrix in the left hand side of (
7.2
) by
Q
. Then
det~\textbf{Q}\neq 0\Leftrightarrow\tau\neq 1(\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2})
. More importantly, if
det~\textbf{Q}\neq 0

and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
, it holds that

w\parallel\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
, and
\gamma\parallel\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0).

This gives the direction of the solution
(w,\gamma)
of
\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
.

Proposition 7.6
.

If
\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
, then

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
possesses exactly two solutions

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)})
and

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)})
,
satisfying

\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|}=\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}|}=-\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}

and

\begin{split}det&~Jac~\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\frac{\pi\tau^{2}(\tau^{3}-1)}{(C_{\tau})^{5}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)(-1)^{m-1},\end{split}

(7.23)

where
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
,
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
and
C_{\tau}:=\tau^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{(1+\tau)^{2}}}
.

Proof.

If
\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
, then
det~\mathbf{Q}\neq 0
and (
7.2
) implies

w\parallel\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\gamma\parallel\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0).

(7.24)

Next we split the proof in three different steps.

Step 1: Computation of
\gamma
in (
7.24
).

By (
7.2
), we write
\gamma=\big(\frac{w}{|w|}\cdot\gamma\big)\frac{w}{|w|}
and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=\big(\frac{w}{|w|}\cdot\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)\frac{w}{|w|}
. Then (
7.2
)
is equivalent to

\begin{split}\frac{{k}\big(|w|,\tau\big)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=&\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\big(w\cdot\gamma\big),\end{split}

(7.25)

and

\begin{split}\frac{\tau{k}\big(|w|,\tau\big)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=&-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{|w|^{2}}\big(w\cdot\gamma\big).\end{split}

(7.26)

Hence from
\tau\times\eqref{sec7-20}-\eqref{sec7-21}
, we get

\begin{split}\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)=-\frac{\beta(\tau+\tau^{2})}{|w|^{2}}\big(w\cdot\gamma\big).\end{split}

(7.27)

Inserting
\frac{w}{|w|}=\pm\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}
into (
7.27
), we find

\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|\cdot|w|=-\frac{\beta(\tau+\tau^{2})}{|w|}\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}\cdot\gamma,

which, together with

\gamma\parallel\,\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
, gives
\gamma=\big(\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}\cdot\gamma\big)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})(1+\tau)|w|^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
.

Step 2: Computation of
w
in (
7.24
).

As stated above, we know that
w\parallel\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
. Hence
w

has exact two directions. The crucial point is to solve the length of
w
.
Inserting (
7.27
) into (
7.25
), we obtain

\begin{split}k\big(|w|,\tau\big)=&\pi d_{\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)~\,\,~~\mbox{with}~~\,\,~d_{\tau}:=\frac{(\tau^{3}-1)}{(1+\tau)\tau}.\end{split}

(7.28)

We have the following alternative.

Case 1.
\frac{w}{|w|}=\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}

In this case, (
7.28
) becomes

\breve{k}_{\varepsilon}(r):=k(r,\tau)-\pi d_{\tau}\big|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big|r\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)=0,

where
k(r,\tau)
is defined in (
7.9
).
Then
\breve{k}_{\varepsilon}(r)=0
possesses exact one solution
r_{\varepsilon}
. In fact, from

\frac{\partial{k}(r,\tau)}{\partial r}>0
, and

\begin{split}\breve{k}_{\varepsilon}(C_{\tau}-|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta})<0,~~\breve{k}_{\varepsilon}(C_{\tau}+|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta})>0,\end{split}

we see that
\breve{k}_{\varepsilon}(r)=0
possesses exact one solution
r_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}
satisfying

r_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}=C_{\tau}+\frac{\pi(C_{\tau})^{2}d_{\tau}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}{1+\tau}\varepsilon^{\beta}+O\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\big),

where
C_{\tau}
and
d_{\tau}
are the constants in Theorem
1.16
and (
7.28
).

Case 2.
\frac{w}{|w|}=-\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}

In this case, (
7.28
) becomes

\begin{split}k(r,\tau)=-d_{\tau}\big|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big|r\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big).\end{split}

(7.29)

In a similar way we deduce that
(
7.29
) has a unique solution
r_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}
satisfying

r_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}=C_{\tau}-\frac{\pi(C_{\tau})^{2}d_{\tau}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}{1+\tau}\varepsilon^{\beta}+O\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\big).

Hence from above discussions, we know that

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
possesses exactly two solutions
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)})
and
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)})
satisfying

\begin{cases}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}=\left[C_{\tau}+\frac{\pi(C_{\tau})^{2}d_{\tau}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}{1+\tau}\varepsilon^{\beta}+O\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\big)\right]\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|},\\[8.53581pt]
\widetilde{\gamma}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}=-\left[\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})(1+\tau)|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(C_{\tau})^{2}}{\tau^{2}}+O\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|\big)\right]\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0),\end{cases}

(7.30)

and

\begin{cases}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}=-\left[C_{\tau}-\frac{\pi(C_{\tau})^{2}d_{\tau}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}{1+\tau}\varepsilon^{\beta}+O\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|^{2}\big)\right]\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|},\\[8.53581pt]
\widetilde{\gamma}^{(2)}_{\varepsilon}=-\left[\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})(1+\tau)|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|(C_{\tau})^{2}}{\tau^{2}}+O\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|\big)\right]\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0).\end{cases}

(7.31)

Step 3: Proof of (
7.23
).

Let
\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
and

\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{{V}}_{\varepsilon,j,1}(w,\gamma),\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,j,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
for
j=1,2
.
Then for
i,j=1,2
, we compute

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=&-\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\delta_{ij}-\left[\frac{1}{|w|\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial{k}(r,\tau)}{\partial r}\big|_{r=|w|}\right.\\[5.69054pt]
&\left.-\frac{2{k}(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+\frac{8(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}+\frac{2\beta(w_{i}\gamma_{j}+w_{j}\gamma_{i})}{|w|^{4}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=-\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\delta_{ij}+\frac{2\beta w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}.\end{split}

By
(
7.25
) and (
7.27
), we have

\frac{k(|\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon})}{|\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}=\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})\frac{(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon})}{|\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}.

(7.32)

Also, using that
w^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}\parallel\widetilde{\gamma}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}
, (
7.9
) and (
7.32
), we obtain

\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}=c_{1}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\delta_{ij}+c_{2}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m)}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}},

with

\begin{cases}c_{1}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{\tau(C_{\tau})^{2}}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big)(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}),\\[5.69054pt]
c_{2}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=-\frac{1+\tau}{(C_{\tau})^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big).\end{cases}

On the other hand, direct computations give

\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}=\underbrace{-\frac{\beta}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}}}_{:=c_{3}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}\delta_{ij}+\underbrace{\frac{2\beta}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}}}_{:=c_{4}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m)}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}}.

Similarly, we compute

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=&\left[\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\delta_{ij}+\left[\frac{\tau}{|w|\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial{k}(r,\tau)}{\partial r}\big|_{r=|w|}\right.\\[5.69054pt]
&\left.-\frac{2{k}(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{8\tau^{2}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\beta(w_{i}\gamma_{j}+w_{j}\gamma_{i})}{|w|^{4}},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=-\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w|^{2}}\delta_{ij}+\frac{2\tau^{2}\beta w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}.\end{split}

So we obtain

\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}=c_{5}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\delta_{ij}+c_{6}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m)}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}},

with

\begin{cases}c_{5}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{(C_{\tau})^{2}}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big)\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon}\big),\\[5.69054pt]
c_{6}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\frac{\tau(1+\tau)}{(C_{\tau})^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big).\end{cases}

We also have

\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})}=c_{7}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\delta_{ij}+c_{8}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m)}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|^{2}},

with

c_{7}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\tau^{2}c_{3}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})
and
c_{8}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\tau^{2}c_{4}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})
.

From the above computations, we have, for
m=1,2
,

\begin{split}&Jac~\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(c_{1}\delta_{ij}+c_{2}\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(c_{3}\delta_{ij}+c_{4}\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[17.07164pt]
\Big(c_{5}\delta_{ij}+c_{6}\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(c_{7}\delta_{ij}+c_{8}\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{array}\right)\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}.\end{split}

Then we get

\begin{split}det&~Jac~\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})=\left[\Big(c_{1}+c_{2}\Big)\times\Big(c_{7}+c_{8}\Big)-\Big(c_{3}+c_{4}\Big)\times\Big(c_{5}+c_{6}\Big)\right]\times\left[c_{1}c_{7}-c_{3}c_{5}\right]\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}.\end{split}

Next, we compute

\displaystyle\left[\Big(c_{3}+c_{4}\Big)\times\Big(c_{5}+c_{6}\Big)-\Big(c_{1}+c_{2}\Big)\times\Big(c_{7}+c_{8}\Big)\right]\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\Big(c_{5}+c_{6}-\tau^{2}\big(c_{1}+c_{2}\big)\Big)\right]\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}=\frac{\tau^{2}}{(C_{\tau})^{4}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O\big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)\Big)<0.

We also have

\begin{split}\left[c_{1}c_{7}-c_{3}c_{5}\right]\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}=&\left[\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\Big(c_{5}-\tau^{2}c_{1}\Big)\right]\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)}\\
=&\frac{\pi(\tau^{3}-1)}{(C_{\tau})^{2}}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}\big)\\
=&\frac{\pi(\tau^{3}-1)}{(C_{\tau})^{2}}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|(-1)^{m-1}.\end{split}

Hence we obtain (
7.23
), which ends the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.7
.

For each solution
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})
of
\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
with
m=1,2
,
letting
\delta>0
small and such that

B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}),\delta\big)\cap B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}),\delta\big)=\emptyset

it holds

\deg\Big({\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)\Big)=\deg\Big(\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)\Big)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{m-1},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau<1,\\[2.84526pt]
(-1)^{m},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau>1.\end{cases}

(7.33)

Hence
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has at least
one solution in
B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
for a small

\delta>0
and
m=1,2
.

Proof.

First, we show that

|\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)|\geq c_{0}>0,\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\;(w,\gamma)\in\partial B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big).

(7.34)

We argue by contradiction and suppose that there are
(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon})\in\partial B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)

such that
|\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon})|\to 0
. Assume
that
(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon})\to(w,\gamma)
and similarly to (
7.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\frac{\beta}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\\[8.53581pt]
\gamma_{\varepsilon,j}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left[\frac{k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon})}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\right]w_{\varepsilon,j}\\[8.53581pt]
\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon})}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\right]w_{\varepsilon,j}\\
\end{array}\right)+o(1).

This gives that

\left|\frac{\tau k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right|\leq C.

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
in (
7.2
) leads to
w,\;\gamma\parallel\,\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
. Moreover, it holds that
k(|w|,\tau)=0
, which implies
|w|=C_{\tau}
.
So we find that
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})\to(w,\gamma)
. This is a contradiction to
(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon})\in\partial B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
.

From (
7.15
) and (
7.34
) we get (
7.33
),
which gives that
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}=0
has a solution in
B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
,
m=1,2
.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

If
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
is a solution of
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then there exists
m\in\{1,2\}
such that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\left(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}|=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big),

(7.41)

where

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)})
are as in (
7.30
) and (
7.31
),
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
with
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
.

Proof.

Let

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
be a solution of
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\frac{\beta}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\\[8.53581pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}&-\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\\[8.53581pt]
\gamma_{\varepsilon,j}\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left[\frac{k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon})}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\right]w_{\varepsilon,j}\\[8.53581pt]
\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon})}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\right]w_{\varepsilon,j}\\
\end{array}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Thus we have that

\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}=\pm\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{|\gamma_{\varepsilon}|}=\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

As in (
7.25
) and (
7.26
) in Proposition
7.6
, we can
derive

\begin{split}\frac{{k}\big(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=&\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)+\frac{\beta}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

(7.42)

and

\begin{split}\frac{\tau{k}\big(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=&-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})}{1+\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.43)

Hence from
\tau\times\eqref{sec7-30}-\eqref{sec7-31}
, we get

\begin{split}\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)=-\frac{\beta(\tau+\tau^{2})}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\big(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.44)

Inserting
\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}=\pm\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)
into (
7.44
), we find

\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|\cdot|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|=-\frac{\beta(\tau+\tau^{2})}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},

which, together with

\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}|}=\pm\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)
, gives

\begin{split}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=&\Big(\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}\cdot\gamma\Big)\frac{\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})(1+\tau)|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

Next we compute the expansion of
\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}
. From
\tau^{2}\times\eqref{sec7-30}+\eqref{sec7-31}
, we get

\begin{split}\frac{k\big(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)}=&\pi d_{\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)~\,\,~~\mbox{with}~~\,\,~d_{\tau}:=\frac{\tau^{3}-1}{(1+\tau)\tau}.\end{split}

That is

\begin{split}k\big(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)=&\pi d_{\tau}\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)+O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta}\right)\\
=&\pi d_{\tau}\big|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big|\cdot\big|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big|\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)+O\left(\varepsilon^{\beta}\right).\end{split}

(7.45)

Also we recall

\begin{split}k\big(|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)=&\pi d_{\tau}\big|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big|\cdot\big|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}\big|\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big).\end{split}

(7.46)

Hence
from
\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}=\frac{\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)
, (
7.45
) and (
7.46
), we have

\begin{split}\frac{k\big(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{k\big(|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau\big)}{|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|}=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big),\end{split}

which gives
|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big)
and then
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\left(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+O\big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big),\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+O\big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)\right)
.
∎

We now consider the non-degeneracy of the solutions of
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
.

Proposition 7.9
.

If
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
is a solution of
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then it holds

\displaystyle det~Jac~{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{\pi\tau^{2}(\tau^{3}-1)}{(C_{\tau})^{5}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)(-1)^{m-1},

(7.47)

where
m\in\{1,2\}
is as in (
7.41
),
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
,
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
and
C_{\tau}:=\tau^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{(1+\tau)^{2}}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.8
, we can consider the case

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\left(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big).

The
computations for the other
case are similar. First, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=&\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\
=&-\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\delta_{ij}-\left[\frac{1}{|w|\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\left.\frac{\partial{k}(r,\tau)}{\partial r}\right|_{r=|w|}\right.\\[5.69054pt]
&\left.-\frac{2{k}(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+\frac{8(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}+\frac{2\beta(w_{i}\gamma_{j}+w_{j}\gamma_{i})}{|w|^{4}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}).\end{split}

By
(
7.42
) and (
7.43
), we have

\frac{k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-2\beta\frac{(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}=\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})\frac{(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon})}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}).

(7.48)

Also, using
\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|}=O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})
and

\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{\gamma}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}|}=O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})

by Lemma
7.8
, (
7.9
) and (
7.48
), we obtain

\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}=\widetilde{c}_{1}\delta_{ij}+\widetilde{c}_{2}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}),

with

\widetilde{c}_{1}=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}{\tau C_{\tau}},\,\,~~\widetilde{c}_{2}=-\frac{1+\tau}{C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big).

On the other hand we can compute

\begin{split}\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}=&\frac{\partial\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon,1,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\
=&\underbrace{-\frac{\beta}{C^{2}_{\tau}}}_{:=\widetilde{c}_{3}}\delta_{ij}+\underbrace{\frac{2\beta}{C^{2}_{\tau}}}_{:=\widetilde{c}_{4}}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}).\end{split}

Similarly, we obtain

\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}=\widetilde{c}_{5}\delta_{ij}+\widetilde{c}_{6}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}),

with

\widetilde{c}_{5}=-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}{C_{\tau}},\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{c}_{6}=\frac{\tau(1+\tau)}{(C_{\tau})^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big).

We also have

\frac{\partial{V}_{\varepsilon,2,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big|_{(w,\gamma)=(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}=\widetilde{c}_{7}\delta_{ij}+\widetilde{c}_{8}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}),

with

\widetilde{c}_{7}=\tau^{2}\widetilde{c}_{3}
and
\widetilde{c}_{8}=\tau^{2}\widetilde{c}_{4}
.
Now denote by
{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,1}
the
2\times 2
matrix

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,1}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|}&-\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|}\\[11.38109pt]
\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|}&\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|}\\
\end{array}\right).

Then it holds

\textbf{Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,1}\left(\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(1)}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(1)}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}|^{2}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\textbf{Q}_{\varepsilon,1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
\end{array}\right).

Hence we have

\begin{split}&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\textbf{Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,1}&\textbf{O}_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\textbf{O}_{2\times 2}&\textbf{Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,1}\\
\end{array}\right)~Jac~{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})~\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\textbf{Q}_{\varepsilon,1}&\textbf{O}_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\textbf{O}_{2\times 2}&\textbf{Q}_{\varepsilon,1}\\
\end{array}\right)\\[8.53581pt]
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\widetilde{c}_{1}+\widetilde{c}_{2}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{3}+\widetilde{c}_{4}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{1}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{3}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
\widetilde{c}_{5}+\widetilde{c}_{6}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{7}+\widetilde{c}_{8}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{5}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\widetilde{c}_{7}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\
\end{array}\right)\\[8.53581pt]
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}-\frac{1+\tau}{C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big)&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\frac{\beta}{C^{2}_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}{\tau C_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&-\frac{\beta}{C^{2}_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\tau(1+\tau)}{(C_{\tau})^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big)&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{C^{2}_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&-\frac{\pi(1+\tau+\tau^{2})|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|}{C_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{C^{2}_{\tau}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

From above computations, we have

\begin{split}det&~Jac~\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{\pi\tau^{2}(\tau^{3}-1)}{(C_{\tau})^{5}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right).\end{split}

Similarly, for the case the case

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\left(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}|=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big),

we can compute

\begin{split}det&~Jac~\widetilde{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=-\frac{\pi\tau^{2}(\tau^{3}-1)\big|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big|}{(C_{\tau})^{5}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\left(1+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\right).\end{split}

∎

Proof of Theorem
1.16
.

The existence of at least two solutions follows from Proposition
7.6
.
Moreover, from (
7.47
), the critical points of

\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
are all nondegenerate. Hence, the number
of solutions are finite.

Next, we prove that for any fixed
m\in\{1,2\}
,
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has a unique solution in

B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
.
For example, suppose that
there are
l
solutions in
B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
. And then
by Proposition
7.9
, we have that

\displaystyle\deg\Big({\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma),0,B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)\Big)=\begin{cases}l\cdot(-1)^{m-1},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau<1,\\[2.84526pt]
l\cdot(-1)^{m},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau>1.\end{cases}

(7.49)

On the other hand, it follows
from (
7.33
) that

\displaystyle\deg\Big({\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma),0,B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)\Big)=\begin{cases}(-1)^{m-1},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau<1,\\[2.84526pt]
(-1)^{m},~~~&\mbox{if}~~~\tau>1,\end{cases}

which, together with (
7.49
), implies that
l=1
.

Moreover, outside of the ball
B\big((\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m)}),\delta\big)
by Proposition
7.6
. the equation
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has no solution.
Therefore, we have shown that
{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has
exactly two solutions.
Moreover, by Proposition
7.6
, these two critical points of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)

are nondegenerate.
∎

Above discussions can also been used to handle the case
\Omega_{\varepsilon}=B(0,1)\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
.

Proof of Theorem
1.21
.

If
\Omega_{\varepsilon}=B(0,1)\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
, then
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{B(0,1)}(0)=0
, and (
7.2
) becomes

\begin{cases}-\frac{\beta\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}=\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}-2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j},\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\beta\tau^{2}\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}=\left[\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}+2\beta\tau^{2}\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j},\end{cases}

(7.50)

for
j=1,2
. Adding
\tau^{2}\times
the first equation of (
7.50
) with the second equation of (
7.50
) yields

k(|w|,\tau)=0
, which gives
|w|=C_{\tau}
. Putting this into the first equation of (
7.50
), we get
\frac{\beta\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}=2\beta\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}w_{j}
, from
which we can derive
\gamma=0
. This shows that if
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
is a critical point of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)
, then letting
(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon})=(\frac{x_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}},\frac{x_{\varepsilon}+\tau y_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}})
, it holds

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}|w_{\varepsilon}|=C_{\tau}~~~\mbox{and}~~~\lim_{\varepsilon\to o}|\gamma_{\varepsilon}|=0~~~\mbox{with}~~~\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{w_{\varepsilon}}{|w_{\varepsilon}|}=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\gamma_{\varepsilon}}{|\gamma_{\varepsilon}|}.

By a suitable rotation, we can assume that
x_{\varepsilon}=(|x_{\varepsilon}|,0)
, Denoting
\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}w_{\varepsilon}=w_{0}
and
\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\gamma_{0}
, we have
w_{0}=(C_{\tau},0)
and
\gamma_{0}=(0,0)
.

We define
{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\big({F}_{\varepsilon,0}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}),{F}_{\varepsilon,1}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}),{F}_{\varepsilon,2}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\big)
with

\begin{cases}{F}_{\varepsilon,0}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{1}}\Big|_{(x,y)=(\varepsilon^{\beta}r,0,\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{1}-r}{\tau},\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{2}}{\tau})},\\[2.84526pt]
{F}_{\varepsilon,j}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=(\varepsilon^{\beta}r,0,\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{1}-r}{\tau},\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{2}}{\tau})},~~~~~\mbox{for}~~~~~j=1,2.\end{cases}

Hence
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})=0
with
x_{\varepsilon}=(|x_{\varepsilon}|,0)
is equivalent to
{\bf F}_{\varepsilon}(\bar{r}_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon,1},\gamma_{\varepsilon,2})=0
with

(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})=(\varepsilon^{\beta}\bar{r}_{\varepsilon},0,\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{\varepsilon,1}-\bar{r}_{\varepsilon}}{\tau},\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma_{\varepsilon,2}}{\tau})
.

Next, we have

\nabla_{(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})}{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\nabla_{(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})}\widetilde{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}&0&0\\
0&\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}&0\\
0&0&\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\\
\end{array}\right)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),

where
\widetilde{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\big(\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,0}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}),\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,1}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}),\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,2}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\big)
with

\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,0}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=-\frac{k(r,\tau)}{r^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}+\frac{\beta\gamma_{1}}{r^{2}},~~\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,1}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=\frac{\beta\tau^{2}\gamma_{1}}{r^{2}},~~~\widetilde{F}_{\varepsilon,2}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}\gamma_{2}}{r^{2}}.

We can verify that
\widetilde{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
has a unique solution
(C_{\tau},0,0)
. And then we deduce that any critical point of
{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=0
belongs to
\mathcal{S}
, with

\mathcal{S}=\left\{(r,\gamma)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2},~|r-C_{\tau}|\leq\varepsilon^{\beta-\delta},~|\gamma|\leq\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|^{1-\delta}}~~~\mbox{for some small fixed}~~~\delta>0\right\}.

Now we calculate

Jac~{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\Big|_{(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{S}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\frac{1+\tau}{r^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\Big(1+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\Big)&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|^{1-\delta}})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{r^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\[8.53581pt]
O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})&-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}}{r^{2}}+O(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|})\\
\end{array}\right).

Hence

{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})
has a unique solution
(r_{\varepsilon,0},\gamma_{\varepsilon,1},\gamma_{\varepsilon,2})
, which tends to
(C_{\tau},0,0)
.

We claim that
\gamma_{\varepsilon,2}=0
. In fact, if
\gamma_{\varepsilon,2}\neq 0
, by
the symmetry of the domain,

(r_{\varepsilon,0},\gamma_{\varepsilon,1},-\gamma_{\varepsilon,2})
is also the solution of
{\bf{F}}_{\varepsilon}(r,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})=0
. This contradicts the uniqueness of the
solution.

Similarly, we can use a suitable rotation to get
y_{\varepsilon}=(|y_{\varepsilon}|,0)
.
Then

x_{\varepsilon}
is uniquely determined with
x_{\varepsilon}=(-|x_{\varepsilon}|,0)
.
So we have proved that up to a rotation,
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)

has exactly two different critical points
if
\Lambda_{1}\neq\Lambda_{2}
, while it has exactly one critical
point if
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
.
Finally, these critical points are nondegenerate in the radial direction.
∎

7.3.
Further expansion of
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)

To study this case
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
or
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
, we need to further expand
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
in Proposition
7.2
and Proposition
7.4
.

Proposition 7.10
.

For
(x,y)\in\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}
and
j=1,2
, it holds

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}&\left\{\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right.\\[2.84526pt]
&\left.\,\,\,+2\pi\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}x_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-(\Lambda_{1}x_{i}+\Lambda_{2}y_{i})\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]\right\}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{split}

(7.51)

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}&\left\{\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}+\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}(y_{j}-x_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right.\\[2.84526pt]
&\left.\,\,\,+2\pi\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}y_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}-(\Lambda_{1}x_{i}+\Lambda_{2}y_{i})\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right]\right\}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{split}

(7.52)

where
\widetilde{h}(x,y):={h}(x,y)+\frac{\pi\big(\Lambda_{1}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot x)+\Lambda_{2}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot y)\big)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}
with
{h}(x,y)
being the function in (
7.4
).

Remark 7.11
.

Now we compare Proposition
7.10
with Proposition
7.2
. The extra terms in the second lines of (
7.51
) and (
7.52
) enable us to determine the direction of the critical point in the case
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
or/and
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
.

Proof of Proposition
7.10
.

To prove this proposition, it suffices to compute the term
\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)
with greater precision than in Proposition
7.2
, as follows:

\begin{split}\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=-\frac{\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|}{2\pi}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|}{2\pi}+(\Lambda_{1}H_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}H_{\Omega}(0,y))}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=-\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)}{2\pi}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Then we get

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+2\pi\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\\
=&-\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)x_{j}}{2\pi|x|^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\\
&-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\sum^{2}_{i=1}x_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Also, by Taylor’s expansion, we have

\begin{split}&\Lambda_{1}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)}{\partial x_{j}}+2\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\\
&=(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+2(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\sum^{2}_{i=1}x_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+2\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}(\Lambda_{1}x_{i}+\Lambda_{2}y_{i})+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}\Big).\end{split}

Hence from above computations, we get

\begin{split}\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=&\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\pi}\left\{-\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}\big(\widetilde{h}(x,y)-\Lambda_{1}-\Lambda_{2}\big)-\frac{\pi(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right.\\[2.84526pt]
&\left.-2\pi\frac{\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\sum^{2}_{i=1}x_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+2\pi\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}(\Lambda_{1}x_{i}+\Lambda_{2}y_{i})\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.53)

Finally, from (
7.5
) and (
7.53
), we prove (
7.51
). In a similar way we deduce (
7.52
).
∎

Proposition 7.12
.

For
(x,y)\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\times\Omega_{\varepsilon}
satisfying

|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
with
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{2}}
and
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
, letting

(w,\gamma)=\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{\beta}},\frac{x+\tau y}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}\right)
,
then it holds

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

(7.54)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}=-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,2,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

(7.55)

where

\displaystyle\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}-1)\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)}{\tau|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(2\beta-\frac{1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,-\frac{\tau\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2}\big)}{(\tau+1)^{3}|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\left[1+\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{(\tau+1)|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[(1+\tau)(\beta-1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\pi\left[\frac{(\tau+1)(\ln|w|+(\beta-1)\ln\varepsilon)-\ln\tau}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}},

(7.56)

and

\displaystyle\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,2,j}(w,\gamma)=

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}-1)\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)}{|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{\tau(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\Big(\frac{2k(|w|,\tau)-1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+2\tau\beta\Big)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}(\tau+2)\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2}\big)}{(\tau+1)|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}+\frac{\tau}{|w|^{2}}\left[\tau\beta+\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{2\beta(\tau+2)(w\cdot\gamma)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{(1+\tau)|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[-\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta-1)}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,+\pi\left[\frac{(\tau+1)(\ln|w|+(\beta-1)\ln\varepsilon)-\ln\tau}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right]\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}

(7.57)

with
k(r,\tau)
being the function in (
7.9
).

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of Proposition
7.4
. Here we need more precise estimates in Taylor’s expansions.
From (
7.51
), we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}&\left\{\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)x_{j}}{\Lambda_{2}|x|^{2}}+\frac{\pi(\tau\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon})}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-\frac{(x_{j}-y_{j})}{|x-y|^{2}}\right.\\[2.84526pt]
&\left.+2\pi\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{\tau\ln\frac{|x|}{\varepsilon}+\ln\frac{|y|}{\varepsilon}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}x_{i}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-(\tau x_{i}+y_{i})\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]\right\}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

(7.58)

Taking
(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)
, then

\begin{split}\frac{\widetilde{h}(x,y)}{\Lambda_{2}}=&\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau+1)+\pi\big(\tau(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot x)+(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot y)\big)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\\
=&\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau+1)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{\pi(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau})(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Expanding (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) to the next order
we get

\begin{split}\frac{\tau\ln|x|+\ln|y|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}=\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta\ln\varepsilon+\ln|w|)-\ln\tau-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

\begin{split}\frac{x_{j}-y_{j}}{|x-y|^{2}}=&\frac{\tau}{(1+\tau)^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}\left[(1+\tau)w_{j}+\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big(\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\gamma_{j}\Big)+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\Big(\frac{(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}|\gamma|^{2})w_{j}-2|w|^{2}(w\cdot\gamma)\gamma_{j}}{(\tau+1)|w|^{4}}\Big)\right]\\
&+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}\Big).\end{split}

Observing that
\tau x_{i}+y_{i}=(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau})\varepsilon^{\beta}w_{i}+O\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}\big)
we prove (
7.54
) by inserting the above computations into (
7.58
).

Proceeding in the same way for (
7.13
) leads to

\begin{split}\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}=&-\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}\left[w_{j}+\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big(\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}-\gamma_{j}\Big)+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\Big(\frac{(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}|\gamma|^{2})w_{j}-2|w|^{2}(w\cdot\gamma)\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{4}}\Big)\right]+O\Big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}\Big),\end{split}

which proves (
7.55
) and ends the proof.
∎

As in (
7.14
), we define

{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\Big(\nabla_{x}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y),\nabla_{y}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)\Big)\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}.

Then Proposition
7.12
gives that

{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}&{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}\\[8.53581pt]
{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}&-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)~~~\mbox{for any}~~~(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},

where

\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)
is defined by

\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\Big(\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\Big)~~~\mbox{with}~~~\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)=\Big(\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,i,1}(w,\gamma),\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,i,2}(w,\gamma)\Big)~~~\mbox{for}~~~i=1,2.

Here
\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,i,1}(w,\gamma)
and
\overline{V}_{\varepsilon,i,2}(w,\gamma)
are the functions in
(
7.12
) and (
7.12
).
Next the analogous of Proposition
7.5
holds.

Proposition 7.13
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
,
it holds

\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\overline{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}&{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}\\[8.53581pt]
{\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}&-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}{\bf{E}}_{2\times 2}\end{array}\right)+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).

Proof.

Recalling (
7.16
) of Proposition
7.5
, we get

\begin{split}\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon,1,j}(w,\gamma)=&\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}\left\{\left[\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)\right]\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

Arguing exactly as in the proof of Proposition
7.5
the claim follows.
∎

7.4.
The case
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
(Proof of Theorem
1.17
)

From now we will use Proposition
7.12
and Proposition
7.13
to prove Theorem
1.17
.
If
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
, then
\tau=1
and
\beta=\frac{1}{4}
. In this
case, the results in Proposition
7.12
can be stated as follows.

Proposition 7.14
.

For
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
,
(x,y)\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\times\Omega_{\varepsilon}
satisfying
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}
, letting

(w,\gamma)=\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}},\frac{x+y}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right)
, then it holds

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w,-\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\gamma\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)-\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2})}{8|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{4|w|^{2}}\left[1+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}+\frac{\pi(4\ln|w|-3\ln\varepsilon)}{2(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-3\pi\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}w_{i}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w,-\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\gamma\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[-\frac{k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2k(|w|)-1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)-\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2})}{8|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}\right.
