\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{|w|^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{4}+\frac{k(|w|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{4|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}+\frac{\pi(4\ln|w|-3\ln\varepsilon)}{2(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+3\pi\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}w_{i}\right\}

\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

where
k(r):=k(r,\tau)|_{\tau=1}=2\ln r+3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)
with
k(r,\tau)
being the function in (
7.9
).

Denote

\begin{cases}l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=-\frac{\pi}{\Lambda_{1}^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w,-\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\gamma\Big)},\\[5.69054pt]
m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=-\frac{\pi}{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w,-\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}w+\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\gamma\Big)}.\end{cases}

First, we give the main part of
l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
.
For any
f(w,\gamma)
, we denote

\partial^{0}f(w,\gamma):=f(w,\gamma),~~\partial^{1}f(w,\gamma):=\nabla_{(w,\gamma)}f(w,\gamma).

(7.59)

Then following result holds.

Lemma 7.15
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{\widetilde{H}}_{\varepsilon}=\Big\{(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},\tau=1\Big\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial^{k}l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}l^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\\[8.53581pt]
\partial^{k}m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}m_{\varepsilon,j}^{*}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{cases}

(7.60)

for
k=0,1
,
j=1,2
, where
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
is the notation in Proposition
7.4
,

\displaystyle l^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)-\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2})}{8|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4|w|^{2}}\left[1+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}+\frac{\pi(4\ln|w|-3\ln\varepsilon)}{2(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-3\pi\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}w_{i},

and

\displaystyle m^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2k(|w|)-1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)-\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2})}{8|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{|w|^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{4}+\frac{k(|w|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{4|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}+\frac{\pi(4\ln|w|-3\ln\varepsilon)}{2(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+3\pi\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}w_{i}.

Proof.

First, by Proposition
7.12
and Proposition
7.14
, we have (
7.60
) with
k=0
.
Also, using Proposition
7.13
, we obtain (
7.60
) with
k=1
.
∎

Now we devote to solving
l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
and
m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
. For this purpose, we introduce following transform firstly.
Let

p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\left[1+\frac{4k(|w|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right]-\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\left[1+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right],

and

q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{1}{2}\Big(l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\Big).

(7.61)

Then we easily get following result.

Lemma 7.16
.

It holds

\begin{cases}l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\
m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\end{cases}\Leftrightarrow\begin{cases}p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\
q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0.\end{cases}

(7.62)

Moreover, if
(w,\gamma)
solves
l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
for
j=1,2
, then it holds

\det\begin{pmatrix}\Big(\frac{\partial l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial l_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial m_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{pmatrix}\neq 0\Leftrightarrow\det\begin{pmatrix}\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{pmatrix}\neq 0.

(7.63)

Now we give the expansion of
p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
.

Proposition 7.17
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{\widetilde{H}}_{\varepsilon}=\Big\{(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},\tau=1\Big\}
, we have

\begin{cases}\partial^{k}p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+\partial^{k}\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\\[5.69054pt]
\partial^{k}q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{cases}

(7.64)

with
k=0,1
,
j=1,2
,
\partial^{k}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
being the notations in (
7.59
) and Proposition
7.4
,

\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\left[\frac{2k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{1/2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma|^{2}}{4|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}-\frac{3\pi(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}-6\pi\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}w_{i},

(7.65)

\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}-\frac{\gamma_{j}}{2|w|^{2}}+3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}},

(7.66)

and

\begin{split}\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=&\Bigg[\Big(\frac{2k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{1/2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}\varepsilon^{1/4}}\big(1-\frac{2}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\big)\Big)w_{j}+\frac{\pi(4\ln|w|-3\ln\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon^{1/4}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\Bigg]\\
&\times\frac{2k(|w|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}.\end{split}

Proof.

First, by (
7.60
), we have

\begin{split}p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)&=\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}l^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\left[1+\frac{4k(|w|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{3\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right]-\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{4}}m^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\left[1+\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{2}}\right]+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)\\
&=\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

Also using (
7.60
), we compute

q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\frac{1}{2}\Big(l^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+m^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\Big)+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)=\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).

These give (
7.64
) with
k=0
. Finally, using Proposition
7.13
, we obtain (
7.64
) with
k=1
.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

Here we point out that the term
\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
is crucial. To estimate
{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
,
(
7.64
) with
k=0
can be written as follows:

\begin{cases}p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\\[5.69054pt]
q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{cases}

But to estimate the derivative of
p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
, the term
\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
in (
7.64
) cannot be ignored.

Proposition 7.19
.

Set

\widetilde{\bf{M}}:=\left[\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{k}}-{3\pi}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial y_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial y_{k}}\right]_{1\leq i,k\leq 2}.

If
\widetilde{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
, then system

\begin{cases}\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\end{cases}

(7.67)

has exactly four solutions
(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
for
m=1,2
, with

\begin{cases}w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}=\pm\left[e^{-\frac{3\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{3\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{2}}\big(9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}+6\pi\lambda_{m}+o(1)\big)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon\right]\nu^{(m)},\\[5.69054pt]
\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon}=6\pi|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)-12\pi\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}\big)w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\end{cases}

(7.68)

where

\nu^{(m)}
is the unit eigenfunction corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda_{m}
of
\widetilde{\bf{M}}
.

Proof.

From (
7.66
) we find

\begin{split}w\cdot\gamma=-6\pi|w|^{2}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w.\end{split}

(7.69)

and then

\begin{split}\gamma=6\pi|w|^{2}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)-12\pi\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w\big)w.\end{split}

(7.70)

Inserting (
7.69
) and (
7.70
) into (
7.65
), for
j=1,2
, we obtain

\begin{split}\left[\frac{2k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}\right]w_{j}=6\pi\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}w\big)_{j}.\end{split}

(7.71)

Thus
w
must be an eigenvector of
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}
has two distinct eigenvalues,
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
, and let

\nu^{(m)}
denote the unit eigenvector corresponding to the eigenvalue
\lambda_{m}
.
Then the eigenvector
w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}
to
(
7.71
) must be proportional to either
\pm\nu^{(1)}
or
\pm\nu^{(2)}
.
Hence by (
7.71
) we get

\begin{split}\frac{2k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}=6\pi\lambda_{m},\end{split}

(7.72)

which has a unique solution satisfying

\begin{split}|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|=e^{-\frac{3\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{3\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{2}}\Big(9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}+6\pi\lambda_{m}\Big)\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon.\end{split}

Therefore, system (
7.67
) admits exactly
four solutions given by (
7.68
).
∎

Let
\check{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big(\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma),\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
.
We have the following results.

Proposition 7.20
.

If
\widetilde{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
, then it holds

\begin{split}&det~Jac~{\bf\check{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{6\pi e^{9\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{m})}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)~~~\mbox{for}~~~m,j=1,2~~~\mbox{and}~~~j\neq m.\end{split}

(7.73)

Proof.

By direct computations, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=&\left[\frac{2k(|w|)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma|^{2}}{4|w|^{4}}\right]\delta_{ij}+\left[\frac{2(|w|\cdot k^{\prime}(|w|)-2k(|w|))}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}+\frac{|\gamma|^{2}}{|w|^{4}}\right]\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\\[2.84526pt]
&-\frac{3\pi\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}+\frac{6\pi(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}-6\pi\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}},\end{split}

(7.74)

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=&-\frac{\gamma_{j}w_{i}}{2|w|^{4}}-\frac{3\pi w_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}},\end{split}

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)\delta_{ij}}{|w|^{4}}+\frac{\gamma_{j}w_{i}+\gamma_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}-\frac{4(w\cdot\gamma)w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{6}},\,\,\,\,\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}-\frac{1}{2|w|^{2}}\delta_{ij}.\end{split}

Now
\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
(see (
7.61
) for the definition of
\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}
) gives

\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}-\frac{\gamma_{j}}{2|w|^{2}}=-3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}
, and then

\begin{split}-\frac{3\pi\gamma_{j}}{|w|^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}+\frac{6\pi(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{|w|^{4}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}=-18\pi^{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}.\end{split}

Inserting this into (
7.74
), we have

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
=&\left[\frac{2k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{4}}\right]{\bf E_{2\times 2}}+\left[\frac{4}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}(1+o(1))\right]\Big(\frac{w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m),\pm}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m),\pm}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[2.84526pt]
&-6\pi{\bf\widetilde{M}}-36\pi^{2}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}.\end{split}

Also inserting (
7.69
) and (
7.70
) into above computations,
we obtain

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=&-\frac{3\pi}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m),\pm}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m),\pm}\Big)+\frac{6\pi\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}\big)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{4}}w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m),\pm}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m),\pm},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma^{(m),\pm}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}=&\frac{6\pi}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}}\Big(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(m),\pm}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(m),\pm}\Big)-\frac{6\pi\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}\big)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}}\delta_{ij}.\end{split}

Now we consider the Jacobian matrix of the vector
\check{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}
at
(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})
. Let

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}:=\left(\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|},\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{w_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}&\frac{w_{\varepsilon,1}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\[11.38109pt]
\frac{w_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}&\frac{w_{\varepsilon,2}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\
\end{array}\right).

Then
{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}
is an orthogonal matrix and satisfies
{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)
and

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\frac{w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(1),+}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\[2.84526pt]
0&0\\
\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}{\bf\widetilde{M}}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=diag\Big(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\Big),\,\,\,\,\,~~~~~\left[\frac{2k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}{4|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{4}}\right]=6\pi\lambda_{1},

from which we get

\begin{split}&{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}\\
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&0\\[8.53581pt]
0&6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})\\
\end{array}\right)-36\pi^{2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left({\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}.\end{split}

Also, we have

\begin{split}&{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left({\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}\\
=&\left(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|},\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\right)^{T}\left(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|},\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\right)\\[5.69054pt]
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|})^{2}&(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|})(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})\\[8.53581pt]
(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|})(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})&(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})^{2}\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Hence it holds

\begin{split}&{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&O(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})-36\pi^{2}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})^{2}\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}w_{\varepsilon,j}^{(1),\pm}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}w_{\varepsilon,i}^{(1),\pm}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left[\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{1}}\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}w_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1),\pm}&w_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1),\pm}\\
\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}w_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1),\pm}\\[5.69054pt]
w_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1),\pm}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{1}}&\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)\right]{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}\\[5.69054pt]
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|&0\\
\end{array}\right)+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\\[5.69054pt]
0\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}&\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}&|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})\\[11.38109pt]
|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})&0\\
\end{array}\right).

This gives

\begin{split}&{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\[11.38109pt]
-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&0\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(7.75)

Moreover, we have

\begin{split}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}&\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\[11.38109pt]
\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&-\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

(7.76)

\begin{split}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}&0\\[5.69054pt]
0&-\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

(7.77)

Thus it holds

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bf Q_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}&\bf O_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\bf O_{2\times 2}&\bf Q^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bf Q_{\varepsilon,2}&\bf O_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\bf O_{2\times 2}&\bf Q_{\varepsilon,2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&O(1)&0&O(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})-36\pi^{2}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})^{2}&-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&0\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}&0\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&O(1)&0&-\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\\[8.53581pt]
\end{array}\right).

And then

\displaystyle det~\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&O(1)&0&O(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})-36\pi^{2}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|})^{2}&-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&0\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}&0\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&O(1)&0&-\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\\[8.53581pt]
\end{array}\right),

which gives (
7.73
) for
m=1
. Similarly it is possible to prove (
7.73
) for
m=2
.
∎

Let
\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big(p_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),p_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma),q_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),q_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
.
We have the following result.

Proposition 7.21
.

For each solution
(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
of
\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
with
m=1,2
, it holds

\deg\Big(\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)\Big)=\deg\Big(\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)\Big)\neq 0,

(7.78)

and problem
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has at least
one solution in
B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)
for a small
\delta>0
.

Proof.

First we have

p_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}+\frac{|k(w)|}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)~~~\mbox{and}~~~q_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

(7.79)

Now for any
(w,\gamma)\in\partial B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}),\delta\big)
for example,
(
7.72
) and the first identity of (
7.79
) gives

\frac{k(|w|)}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}|w-w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=O\left(1+\frac{|k(w)|}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

(7.80)

We claim that

\frac{k(w)}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\ln\varepsilon|}=O(1).

(7.81)

Otherwise,
\frac{k(w)}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\ln\varepsilon|}\to\infty
and
then (
7.80
) implies
|w-w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\to 0
.

On the other hand, by Taylor’s expansion, (
7.72
) and the second identity of (
7.79
), we know

|\gamma-\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=O\Big(|w-w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\Big)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)=o(1).

This is a contradiction with
(w,\gamma)\in\partial B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}),\delta\big)
. Hence (
7.81
) holds.

Now for any
t\in[0,1]
, it holds

t\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)+(1-t)\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\check{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}+\frac{|k(w)|}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\neq 0,\hskip 9.24994pt\forall(w,\gamma)\in\partial B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big).

Then as in the proof of Theorem
1.16
, we obtain a contradiction.
Therefore, (
7.78
) follows, which implies that the problem
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
admits at least
one solution in
B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)
for some small
\delta>0
.
∎

Next result is the analogous of Lemma
7.8
.

Lemma 7.22
.

If
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
is a solution of
\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then there exists
m\in\{1,2\}
such that

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}+o\big(1\big),\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}+o\big(1\big)\Big)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)

or

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}+o\big(1\big),\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}+o\big(1\big)\Big)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big),

where

(w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
are as in (
7.68
).

Proof.

Let

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
be a solution of
\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
. Then

\begin{split}\left[\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma|^{2}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\right]\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}=3\pi(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}+6\pi\sum^{2}_{i=1}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\end{split}

(7.82)

and

\begin{split}&\frac{(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}-\frac{\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon,j}}{2|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}+3\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}=O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.83)

From
\displaystyle\sum^{2}_{j=1}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}\times\eqref{sec7-68}
, we have

\begin{split}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=-6\pi|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{split}

(7.84)

and then

\begin{split}\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=6\pi|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)-12\pi\big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\big)\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.85)

Inserting (
7.84
) and (
7.85
) into (
7.82
), we get

\begin{split}\left[\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}\right]\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}=6\pi\Big(\widetilde{\bf{M}}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\Big)_{j}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

Let
\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}\to\eta
. Then there exists
m\in\{1,2\}
such that
\eta=\nu^{(m)}
or
\eta=-\nu^{(m)}
. Thus,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left[\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}\right]=6\pi\lambda_{m}.

Without loss of generality, we suppose

\eta=\nu^{(1)}=\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}
, then it holds

\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}\to 0

and

\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}+6\pi\lambda_{1}+o(1).

(7.86)

Also we recall

\begin{split}\frac{2k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-9\pi^{2}|\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)|^{2}=6\pi\lambda_{1}.\end{split}

(7.87)

Hence from
(
7.86
) and (
7.87
), we get

\begin{split}\frac{k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=o(1).\end{split}

This gives
|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)
and then
|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}-w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o(1)
by

\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}\to 0
.
∎

We now consider the non-degeneracy of the solutions of
\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
.

Proposition 7.23
.

If
\widetilde{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
and
(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
is a solution of
\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then it holds

\displaystyle det~Jac~\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{6\pi e^{9\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{m})}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)\neq 0~~~\mbox{with}~~~j=1,2~~~\mbox{and}~~~j\neq m.

(7.88)

Proof.

By Lemma
7.22
, we just consider the case

\displaystyle(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(1\big),\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(1\big)\Big)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big).

(7.89)

The computations for the other one are similar. First, by (
7.64
), we have

\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}+\frac{\partial\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

By (
7.74
) and (
7.89
), we have

\begin{split}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
=&\left[\frac{2k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|)}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{|\gamma^{(1),+}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{4}}\right]{\bf E_{2\times 2}}+\left[\frac{4}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}(1+o(1))\right]\Big(\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[2.84526pt]
&-6\pi{\bf\widetilde{M}}-36\pi^{2}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}o(1)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&o(1)\\
\end{array}\right)\\[5.69054pt]
=&6\pi\lambda_{1}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1+o(1)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&1+o(1)\\
\end{array}\right)+\left[\frac{4}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}(1+o(1))\right]\Big(\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[2.84526pt]
&-6\pi{\bf\widetilde{M}}-36\pi^{2}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}o(1)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&o(1)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Next compute

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}=&\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\left[\frac{2k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}\big(1-\frac{2}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\big)\right]\delta_{ij}\\
&-\left[\frac{2(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})k^{\prime}(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{3}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}(1+o(1))\right]\Big(\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
&+\frac{\pi(4\ln|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|-3\ln\varepsilon)k^{\prime}(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}.\end{split}

We take
\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}
being an orthogonal matrix such that

{\bf\widetilde{Q}}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~\Big({\bf\widetilde{Q}}_{\varepsilon,2}-{\bf{Q}}_{\varepsilon,2}\Big)_{ij}=o\big(1\big),

where
\bf Q_{\varepsilon,2}
is the orthogonal matrix in the proof of Proposition
7.20
. Then it holds

\begin{split}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{array}\right)\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&O(1)\\[5.69054pt]
O\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)&6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})-36\pi^{2}(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})^{2}+o(1)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Also we recall

\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}+\frac{\partial\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

By direct computations, (
7.89
) and the fact that
k(|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|)=O\big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}|\ln\varepsilon|\big)
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\widetilde{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}:=&-\Big(\frac{k(|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\big(1-\frac{2}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\big)\Big)\frac{\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big).\end{split}

Then using (
7.89
), it holds

\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)=\frac{\partial\bar{p}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}+o\big(1\big).

(7.90)

Hence from (
7.75
) and (
7.90
), we get

\begin{split}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{array}\right)\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}o(1)&-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}+o(1)\\[11.38109pt]
-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}+o(1)&o(1)\\
\end{array}\right)\end{split}

and

\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial w_{j}}+o\big(1\big),\,\,\,\,~~~~~\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{q}_{\varepsilon,i}(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}+o\big(1\big).

Then using the above estimate, (
7.76
) and (
7.77
), we have

\begin{split}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{array}\right)\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o\big(1\big)&\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}+o\big(1\big)\\[11.38109pt]
\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}+o\big(1\big)&-\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o\big(1\big)\\
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

\begin{split}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,i}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\end{array}\right)\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o\big(1\big)&o\big(1\big)\\[5.69054pt]
o\big(1\big)&-\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o\big(1\big)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

So we have proved that

\begin{split}&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{T}&\bf O_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\bf O_{2\times 2}&\bf\widetilde{Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,j}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial p_{\varepsilon,j}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,j}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial q_{\varepsilon,j}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}&\bf O_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\bf O_{2\times 2}&\bf\widetilde{Q}_{\varepsilon,2}\\
\end{array}\right)\\[8.53581pt]
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{4e^{{3\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}}}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)&O(1)&o(1)&O(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{4}}}\right)&a_{\varepsilon}+o(1)&-\frac{3\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&o(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&\frac{6\pi(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+})}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),\pm}|\cdot|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}&\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o(1)&o(1)\\[8.53581pt]
O(1)&O(1)&o(1)&-\frac{1}{2|w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}+o(1)\\[8.53581pt]
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

with
a_{\varepsilon}:=6\pi(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2})-36\pi^{2}\Big(\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\cdot\frac{w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|w_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\Big)^{2}
. This shows

\displaystyle det~Jac~\hat{\bf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{6\pi e^{9\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{m})}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)\neq 0,

for the case

(\widetilde{w}_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(w_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(1\big),\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(1\big)\Big)

which proves (
7.88
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.17
.

The existence of at least four solutions follows from Proposition
7.21
and (
7.62
).
Also from (
7.62
), (
7.63
) and (
7.88
), the critical points of

\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
are all nondegenerate. Therefore, the number
of solutions is finite.

Next, we prove that for any fixed
m\in\{1,2\}
,
\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has a unique solution in

B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}),\delta\big)
or

B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}),\delta\big)
.
For instance, suppose that there are
l
solutions in
B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}),\delta\big)
.
Then, by Proposition
7.23
, we have

\displaystyle\deg\Big(\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma),0,B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}),\delta\big)\Big)=l\,\text{sign}(\lambda_{m}-\lambda_{j}),\hskip 9.24994ptj\neq m.

(7.91)

On the other hand, it follows from (
7.73
) and (
7.78
) that

\displaystyle\deg\Big(\hat{\mathbf{V}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma),0,B\big((w_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+},\gamma_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}),\delta\big)\Big)=\text{sign}(\lambda_{m}-\lambda_{j}),\hskip 9.24994ptj\neq m,

which, together with (
7.91
), implies that
l=1
.

Hence, we have proved that
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
possesses exactly four type III critical points. Since
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
in the expression of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
, then

\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(y,x)
. Hence if
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
is a
critical point of
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})=0
is equivalent to
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(y_{\varepsilon},x_{\varepsilon})=0
. This means that only two of them are nontrivially distinct.
∎

Remark 7.24
.

Now we give a domain on which the assumptions of Theorem
1.17
hold.
For example, let
\Omega=B(Q,1)
with
0<|Q|<1
. Then
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\neq 0
.
Moreover, by direct computation we obtain

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{k}}\Big|_{x=y}=\frac{|y-Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|y-Q|^{2})^{2}}\left(\delta_{ik}-\frac{2(y_{i}-Q_{i})(y_{k}-Q_{k})}{|y-Q|^{2}}\right),~~~\text{for }i,k=1,2,

and

\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{i}}=-\frac{2(y_{i}-Q_{i})}{2\pi(1-|y-Q|^{2})},~~~\text{for }i=1,2.

Hence, we have

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{k}}-3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{B(Q,1)}(0)}{\partial y_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{B(Q,1)}(0)}{\partial y_{k}}=\frac{|Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|Q|^{2})^{2}}\left(\delta_{ik}-\frac{8Q_{i}Q_{k}}{|Q|^{2}}\right),~~~\text{for }i,k=1,2.

Recalling that

\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}:=\left[\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{k}}-3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial y_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}{\partial y_{k}}\right]_{1\leq i,k\leq 2},

we find that the two eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}
are

\frac{|Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|Q|^{2})^{2}}
and
-\frac{7|Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|Q|^{2})^{2}}
.

Furthermore, by a perturbation argument, in the ellipse

\Omega_{\delta}=\Big\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},(x_{1}-Q_{1})^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{1}\delta\big)^{2}+(x_{2}-Q_{2})^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{2}\delta\big)^{2}<1\Big\},

where
Q=(Q_{1},Q_{2})\in B(0,1)
,
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\geq 0,\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
,
and
\delta>0
is small, we can show that the corresponding matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}

has two different eigenvalues

\frac{|Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|Q|^{2})^{2}}+o_{\delta}(1)
and
-\frac{7|Q|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|Q|^{2})^{2}}+o_{\delta}(1)
.

7.5.
The case
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
(Proof of Theorem
1.19
)

We now use Proposition
7.12
to study the case

\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
. In this case
(
7.54
) and (
7.55
) become

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\left(2\beta-\frac{1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)-\frac{\tau\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2}\big)}{(\tau+1)^{3}|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\left[1+\frac{2(w\cdot\gamma)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{(\tau+1)|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}+2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[(1+\tau)(\beta-1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

(7.92)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{\tau(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\Big(\frac{2k(|w|,\tau)-1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+2\tau\beta\Big)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\frac{\beta\tau^{2}(\tau+2)\varepsilon^{\beta}\big(4(w\cdot\gamma)^{2}-|w|^{2}\cdot|\gamma|^{2}\big)}{(\tau+1)|w|^{6}}\right]w_{j}+\frac{\tau}{|w|^{2}}\left[\tau\beta+\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{2\beta(\tau+2)(w\cdot\gamma)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{(1+\tau)|w|^{2}}\right]\gamma_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta-1)}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

(7.93)

Let
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
be a Type III critical point of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
, and define

(w_{\varepsilon},\gamma_{\varepsilon}):=\Big(\frac{x_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}},\frac{x_{\varepsilon}+\tau y_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}\Big)
, then we have that
\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}|\gamma_{\varepsilon}|<\infty
.
However, if
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
, it is possible to deduce a more precise estimate.

Proposition 7.25
.

If
\nabla\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)=0
, then it holds

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{|\gamma_{\varepsilon}|}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}<\infty,

(7.94)

where
\gamma_{\varepsilon}:=\frac{x_{\varepsilon}+\tau y_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}}
with

(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
being the type III critical point of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
.

Proof.

From (
7.5
)
\times\tau-
(
7.5
), we have

\frac{k(|w_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big)
.
Putting this into (
7.5
), we get

-\frac{(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\gamma_{\varepsilon})}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}\left(2\beta-\frac{1}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right)w_{\varepsilon,j}+\frac{\beta}{|w_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}\gamma_{\varepsilon,j}=O\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\Big),

which gives (
7.94
).
∎

Now using Proposition
7.25
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{2\beta(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}+2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[(1+\tau)(\beta-1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi}\left\{\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{2\tau^{2}\beta(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}-2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta-1)}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}\right\}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Define

\begin{cases}f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=-\frac{\pi}{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)},\\[5.69054pt]
g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=-\frac{\pi}{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=\Big(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau}\Big)}.\end{cases}

(7.95)

First, we give the main part of
f_{\varepsilon,j}
and
g_{\varepsilon,j}
.

Lemma 7.26
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{*}=\Big\{(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{|\gamma|}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}<\infty\Big\}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial^{k}f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}f^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\\[8.53581pt]
\partial^{k}g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}g_{\varepsilon,j}^{*}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{cases}

(7.96)

with
k=0,1
,
j=1,2
,
\partial^{k}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
being the notations in (
7.59
) and Proposition
7.4
,

\displaystyle f^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{2\beta(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}+2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[(1+\tau)(\beta-1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i},

and

\displaystyle g^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=

\displaystyle\left[-\frac{\tau k(|w|,\tau)}{|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{2\tau^{2}\beta(w\cdot\gamma)}{|w|^{4}}\right]w_{j}

\displaystyle+\frac{\tau^{2}\beta}{|w|^{2}}\gamma_{j}-2\pi\varepsilon^{\beta}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\left[\frac{(1+\tau)(\beta-1)}{\tau}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\big(\tau-\frac{1}{\tau}\big)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]w_{i}.

Proof.

First, (
7.96
) with
k=0
holds by Proposition
7.12
and Proposition
7.25
.
Also using Proposition
7.13
, we can deduce (
7.96
) with
k=1
.
∎

Now we devote to solve
f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
and
g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
. This is tedious and we introduce following transform firstly.
Let

\begin{cases}h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{1}{\tau(\tau+1)\varepsilon^{\beta}}\Big(\tau^{2}f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)-g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\Big),\\[5.69054pt]
n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{1}{2\beta\tau(\tau+1)\varepsilon^{\beta}}\Big(\tau f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)\Big).\end{cases}

Then we have following results directly.

Lemma 7.27
.

It holds

\begin{cases}f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\end{cases}\Leftrightarrow~~~\begin{cases}h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0.\end{cases}

(7.97)

Moreover, if
(w,\gamma)
solves
f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
for
j=1,2
, then it holds

det~\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial f_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial g_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)\neq 0\Leftrightarrow~~~det~\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)}{\partial\gamma_{i}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)\neq 0.

Now we give the expansion of
h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
.

Proposition 7.28
.

For any
(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{*}=\Big\{(w,\gamma)\in\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon},\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{|\gamma|}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}<\infty\Big\}
, we have

\begin{cases}\partial^{k}h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+\partial^{k}h^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\\[5.69054pt]
\partial^{k}n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\partial^{k}\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+\partial^{k}n^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{cases}

(7.98)

with
k=0,1
,
j=1,2
,
\partial^{k}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}
being the notations in (
7.59
) and Proposition
7.4
,

\begin{cases}\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right]w_{j}-\frac{2\pi}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}\Big(\overline{\bf{M}}w\Big)_{j},\\[5.69054pt]
h^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{(\tau-1)(w\cdot\gamma)}{\tau|w|^{4}\varepsilon^{\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}w_{j},\end{cases}

and

\begin{cases}\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=\frac{(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{4}}-\frac{\gamma_{j}}{2\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{2}}-\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}-1)}{\tau^{3}}\Big({\bf{M}}_{1}w\Big)_{j},\\
n^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma):=-\frac{(\tau+1)(w\cdot\gamma)w_{j}}{\tau\varepsilon^{\beta}|w|^{4}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))},\end{cases}

with

\overline{\bf{M}}:=\left[(\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}

and

{\bf{M}}_{1}:=\left[(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+(\tau+1)^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}
.

Proof.

First, (
7.98
) with
k=0
holds by Lemma
7.26
and Lemma
7.27
. Next, using Proposition
7.13
, we can get (
7.98
) with
k=1
.
∎

Remark 7.29
.

Here we point out that the terms
{h}^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
{n}^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
are crucial. To estimate
{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
,
(
7.98
) with
k=0
can be written as follows:

\begin{cases}h_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\\[5.69054pt]
n_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)+O\Big(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{cases}

But to estimate the derivative of
{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
, the terms
{h}^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
and
{n}^{*}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
in (
7.98
) cannot be ignored.

Proposition 7.30
.

If
\overline{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}

with unit eigenvectors
v^{(1)}
and
v^{(2)}
, then system

\begin{cases}\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\\[2.84526pt]
\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0,\end{cases}

(7.99)

has exactly four
solutions
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
with

\begin{cases}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}=\pm\left[C_{\tau}+\frac{\pi C_{\tau}^{3}\mu_{m}}{(\tau^{2}+\tau)}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon\big(1+o(1)\big)\right]v^{(m)},\\[5.69054pt]
\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}=-\frac{2\pi(\tau^{2}-1)|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}{\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}+\frac{4\pi(\tau^{2}-1)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}{\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}\Big)\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\end{cases}

(7.100)

where
C_{\tau}=\tau^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{(1+\tau)^{2}}}
is the constant in Theorem
1.13
,
\tau=\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{\Lambda_{2}}
and
\beta=\frac{\tau}{(\tau+1)^{2}}
.

Proof.

Since

\overline{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}

with unit eigenvectors
v^{(1)}
and
v^{(2)}
, then

\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=\frac{2\pi\mu_{i}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)},\;\;\frac{w}{|w|}=\pm v^{(i)},\hskip 9.24994pti=1,2,

which allows to compute
|w|
as follows,

|w|=C_{\tau}+\frac{\pi\mu_{i}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon\big(1+o(1)\big)}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}.

Since
\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)
is independent of
\gamma
, then

\widetilde{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w):=\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
has four solutions

\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}=\pm\left[C_{\tau}+\frac{\pi C_{\tau}^{3}\mu_{m}}{(\tau^{2}+\tau)}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon\big(1+o(1)\big)\right]v^{(m)}.

On the other hand,
\sum^{2}_{j=1}w_{j}\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
gives

\displaystyle w\cdot\gamma=\frac{2\pi(\tau^{2}-1)|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}\Big(w{\bf{M}}_{1}w\Big).

Inserting this
into
\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
, we can uniquely determine
\gamma
by
w
,

\displaystyle\gamma=-\frac{2\pi(\tau^{2}-1)|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}{\bf{M}}_{1}w+\frac{4\pi(\tau^{2}-1)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}\Big(w{\bf{M}}_{1}w\Big)w.

This
shows that (
7.99
) has exactly four
solutions as in (
7.100
).
∎

Let

{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big(\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma),\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
.
We have the following results.

Proposition 7.31
.

If
\overline{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
, then it holds

\displaystyle det~Jac~{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})=\frac{\pi(\mu_{j}-\mu_{m})}{2\tau^{2}C_{\tau}^{6}\varepsilon^{4\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)\neq 0~~~\mbox{with}~~~j=1,2~~~\mbox{and}~~~j\neq m,

(7.101)

where
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
with
m=1,2
are all solutions of
{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
and
C_{\tau}=\tau^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)(\tau^{2}+\tau+1)}{(1+\tau)^{2}}}
is the constant in Theorem
1.13
.

Proof.

By direct computations,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{w_{j}}}=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{k(|w|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right]\delta_{ij}-\frac{2\pi}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}\overline{\bf{M}}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|w|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\Big(|w|\cdot\frac{\partial k(|w|,\tau)}{\partial r}-2k(|w|,\tau)\Big)\right]\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{2}},

(7.102)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{\gamma_{j}}}=0,~~~~~\frac{\partial\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{\gamma_{j}}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{ij}}{2|w|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}-\frac{w_{i}w_{j}}{|w|^{4}\varepsilon^{\beta}}.

Since
\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{\gamma_{j}}}=0
, then

\displaystyle det~\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{w_{j}}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{\gamma_{j}}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{w_{j}}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{\gamma_{j}}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)=det\left(\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{w_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\cdot det\left(\frac{\partial\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial{w_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}.

Now we consider the Jacobian matrix of
{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}
at
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})
and denote by

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}:=\left(\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|},\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}&\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(2),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\[11.38109pt]
\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}&\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(2),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(2),+}|}\\
\end{array}\right).

Then
{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}
is an orthogonal matrix and satisfies
{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,3}\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)
.
Hence we have

\displaystyle{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\left(\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,i}^{(1),+}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,j}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\[2.84526pt]
0&0\\
\end{array}\right).

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,3}{\bf\overline{M}}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}=diag\Big(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\Big),\,\,\,\,\,~~~~~\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=\frac{2\pi\mu_{1}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}.

So we can get

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,3}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial{w_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{(1+\tau)(1+o(1))}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}}&0\\[11.38109pt]
0&\frac{2\pi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}\\
\end{array}\right)

and

\displaystyle{\bf Q}^{T}_{\varepsilon,3}\left(\frac{\partial\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+})}{\partial{w_{j}}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{2|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}&0\\[11.38109pt]
0&\frac{1}{2|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}\\
\end{array}\right).

Hence we deduce (
7.101
) for
m=1
. In a similar way we get (
7.101
) for
m=2
.
∎

Let

\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=\big(h_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),h_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma),n_{\varepsilon,1}(w,\gamma),n_{\varepsilon,2}(w,\gamma)\big)
.
We have following result.

Proposition 7.32
.

For each solution
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
of
{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
with
m=1,2
, it holds

\deg\Big(\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)\Big)=\deg\Big({\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon},0,B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)\Big)\neq 0,

(7.103)

and problem
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has at least
one solution in
B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)
for a small

\delta>0
.

Proof.

First, we have

\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)={\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Then for any
t\in[0,1]
, it holds

t{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)+(1-t)\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)={\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\neq 0,\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\;(w,\gamma)\in\partial B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big).

This, together with (
7.101
), gives (
7.103
). So the equation
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
has at least
one solution in
B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm}),\delta\big)
for a small

\delta>0
.
∎

Lemma 7.33
.

If
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})

is a solution of
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then there exists
m\in\{1,2\}
such that

\displaystyle(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}+o\big(1\big),\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}+o\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\big)\Big)~~~\mbox{and}~~~|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big),

(7.104)

or

\displaystyle(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}+o\big(1\big),\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}+o\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\big)\Big)~~~\mbox{and}~~~|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big),

(7.105)

where

(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),\pm})
are as in (
7.100
).

Proof.

Let

(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
be a solution of
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
. Then

\begin{split}\left[\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right]\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,j}-\frac{2\pi}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}\Big(\overline{\bf{M}}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\Big)_{j}=O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\end{split}

(7.106)

and

\begin{split}&\frac{(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{4}}-\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon,j}}{2\varepsilon^{\beta}|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}}-\frac{\pi(\tau^{2}-1)}{\tau^{3}}\Big({\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\Big)_{j}=O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

(7.107)

Let
\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|}\to\eta
. Then from (
7.106
), there exists
m\in\{1,2\}
such that either
\eta=v^{(m)}
or
\eta=-v^{(m)}
. Thus,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\left[\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right]=\frac{2\pi\mu_{m}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}.

Without loss of generality, we suppose that

\eta=v^{(1)}=\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}
, then it holds

\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}\to 0

and

\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=\frac{2\pi\mu_{m}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}+o(1).

(7.108)

Also we recall

\begin{split}\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=\frac{2\pi\mu_{m}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}.\end{split}

(7.109)

Hence from
(
7.108
) and (
7.109
), we get

\begin{split}\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}-\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=o(1).\end{split}

This gives that
|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)
and then
|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o(1)
by

\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|}-\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|}\to 0
.

On the other hand, from
\sum^{2}_{j=1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,j}\times\eqref{sec7-94}
, we have

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\cdot\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\frac{2\pi(\tau^{2}-1)|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}{\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\Big)+O\big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big).

Inserting this into
\bar{n}_{\varepsilon,j}(w,\gamma)=0
we get

\displaystyle\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=-\frac{2\pi(\tau^{2}-1)|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}{\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}+\frac{4\pi(\tau^{2}-1)\varepsilon^{\beta}}{\tau^{3}}\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}{\bf{M}}_{1}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}\Big)\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}+O\big(\frac{\varepsilon^{\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big),

which, together with
|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o(1)
, gives
|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\big)
.
∎

We now consider the non-degeneracy of the solutions of
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
.

Proposition 7.34
.

If
\overline{\bf{M}}
has two different eigenvalues
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
,
(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})
is a solution of
\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(w,\gamma)=0
, then it holds

\displaystyle det~Jac~\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{\pi(\mu_{j}-\mu_{m})}{2\tau^{2}C_{\tau}^{6}\varepsilon^{4\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)\neq 0~~~\mbox{with}~~~j=1,2~~~\mbox{and}~~~j\neq m,

(7.110)

where
m\in\{1,2\}
is such that (
7.104
) or (
7.105
) holds.

Proof.

Recalling (
7.98
) we have

\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}=\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}+\frac{\partial{h}^{*}_{\varepsilon,i}(w,\gamma)}{\partial w_{j}}+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Since
|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}|=O(\varepsilon^{\beta})
by (
7.100
) and Lemma
7.33
, we get

\frac{\partial{h}^{*}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}=O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

(7.111)

Now we will carry out the computations only at

(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(1\big),\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o\big(\varepsilon^{\beta}\big)\Big)
. The computations for the other
cases are similar. We take
\bf\overline{Q}_{\varepsilon,3}
being an orthogonal matrix such that

{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\\
\end{array}\right)~~~\mbox{and}~~~\Big({\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}-{\bf Q}_{\varepsilon,3}\Big)_{ij}=o\big(1\big),

where
\bf Q_{\varepsilon,3}
is the orthogonal matrix in the proof of Proposition
7.31
.
Now using Lemma
7.33
, we have
|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|-|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}|=o\big(\varepsilon^{2\beta}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\big)
. And then by (
7.111
), we get
we get

\begin{split}&{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}={\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial\overline{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)&O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\\[5.69054pt]
O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)&O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Also, by (
7.5
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial{w_{j}}}=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\right]\delta_{ij}-\frac{2\pi}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}\overline{\bf{M}}_{ij}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2\beta}|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}\Big(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|\cdot\frac{\partial k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{\partial r}-2k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)\Big)\right]\frac{\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,i}\overline{w}_{\varepsilon,j}}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}},

and
