\displaystyle{\bf\overline{Q}}^{T}_{\varepsilon,3}~{\bf\overline{M}}~{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mu_{1}+o(1)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&\mu_{2}+o(1)\\
\end{array}\right),\,\,\,\,\,~~~~~\frac{k(|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|,\tau)}{|\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}|^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))}=\frac{2\pi\mu_{1}}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}+o(1).

Hence it holds

\begin{split}&{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{(\tau+1)}{C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon}(1+o(1))&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&\frac{2\pi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}+o(1)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Also, we can compute

\begin{split}&{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}\\
=&\underbrace{{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial\overline{h}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}}_{={\bf{O}}_{2\times 2}}+\underbrace{{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial{h}^{*}_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}}_{=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}O\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\beta}|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)&0\\[5.69054pt]
0&0\\
\end{array}\right)}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)&O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\\[5.69054pt]
O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)&O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)\\
\end{array}\right)\\
=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}\right)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&o(1)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

Similarly, by direct computations, we have

\begin{split}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}O\big(1\big)&O\big(1\big)\\[5.69054pt]
O\big(1\big)&O\big(1\big)\\
\end{array}\right)\end{split}

and

\begin{split}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}=&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{2C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}\big(1+o(1)\big)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&\frac{1}{2C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}+o(1)\\
\end{array}\right).\end{split}

That is

\begin{split}&\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}&{\bf O}_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
{\bf O}_{2\times 2}&{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}^{T}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\[11.38109pt]
\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial w_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}&\Big(\frac{\partial n_{\varepsilon,i}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})}{\partial\gamma_{j}}\Big)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\\
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}&{\bf O}_{2\times 2}\\[11.38109pt]
{\bf O}_{2\times 2}&{\bf\overline{Q}}_{\varepsilon,3}\\
\end{array}\right)\\[8.53581pt]
&=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{(\tau+1)}{C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon}(1+o(1))&o(1)&o\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\beta}}\right)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o(1)&\frac{2\pi(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}{\tau^{2}(\tau+1)}+o(1)&o(1)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
O(1)&O(1)&\frac{1}{2C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}\big(1+o(1)\big)&o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
O(1)&O(1)&o(1)&-\frac{1}{2C_{\tau}^{2}\varepsilon^{\beta}}+o(1)\\[5.69054pt]
\end{array}\right),\end{split}

which gives

\displaystyle det~Jac~\hat{\bf{W}}_{\varepsilon}(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\frac{\pi(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})}{2\tau^{2}C_{\tau}^{6}\varepsilon^{2\beta}\ln\varepsilon}\big(1+o(1)\big)\neq 0,

for the case

(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon})=\Big(\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o(1),\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(1),+}+o(1)\Big)
.
And then
(
7.110
) holds for
m=1,2
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.19
.

The proof is very similar to that of Theorem
1.17
.

First, (
7.95
), (
7.97
), Proposition
7.30
and Proposition
7.31
give us that

\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
has at least four critical points, which are all nondegenerate.

Also combining Proposition
7.31
, Proposition
7.32
and Proposition
7.34
, we deduce that
for any fixed
m\in\{1,2\}
,

\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)\Big|_{(x,y)=(\varepsilon^{\beta}w,\frac{-\varepsilon^{\beta}w+\varepsilon^{2\beta}\gamma}{\tau})}=0

has a unique solution on

B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),+}),\delta\big)
or

B\big((\overline{w}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-},\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{(m),-}),\delta\big)
. And then
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
has exactly four type III critical points. Moreover, if
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
, only two of them are nontrivially different.
∎

Appendix A
Basic estimates for Kirchhoff-Routh function

In this section, we establish some estimates for the Kirchhoff-Routh function
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
.
For this purpose, it is crucial to estimate the regular part of the
Green’s function and its derivatives.

A.1.
Estimates for regular part of the
Green function

Lemma A.1
.

Let
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
, it holds

\begin{split}H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)+H_{\Omega}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}{\varepsilon}-\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}+O(\varepsilon).\end{split}

(A.1)

In particular, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x)=&\mathcal{R}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x)+\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{|x-P|^{2}}{\varepsilon}-\frac{2\pi(G_{\Omega}(x,P))^{2}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+O(\varepsilon).\end{split}

(A.2)

Proof.

First, we define

b_{\varepsilon}(x,y):=H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)-H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)-H_{\Omega}(x,y)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}{\varepsilon}+\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}.

Then
\Delta_{x}b_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=0
. For
x\in\partial\Omega
, we have

H_{\Omega}(x,y)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln|x-y|~~\mbox{and}~~G_{\Omega}(x,P)=0
. Hence for
x\in\partial\Omega
, it holds

\begin{split}b_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=&G_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln|x-y|-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}{\varepsilon}\\
=&\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\ln\sqrt{\frac{|x-P|^{2}\cdot|y-P|2}{\varepsilon^{2}}-2(x-P)\cdot(y-P)+\varepsilon^{2}}-\ln\frac{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}{\varepsilon}\right)\\
=&O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}\right)=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|}\right).\end{split}

Also for
x\in\partial B(P,\varepsilon)
,

\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi H_{\Omega}(P,P)}=\frac{-\ln\varepsilon-2\pi H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi H_{\Omega}(P,P)}=-1+O\Big(\frac{|x-P|}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big)=-1+O\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),

and then

\begin{split}b_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=&-H_{\Omega}(x,y)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln|y-P|-G_{\Omega}(P,y)+O(\varepsilon)=-H_{\Omega}(x,y)+H_{\Omega}(P,y)+O(\varepsilon)=O(\varepsilon).\end{split}

Hence by the maximum principle we deduce that
b_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=O(\varepsilon)
for
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
. Thus (
A.1
) holds. Finally,
letting
y=x
in (
A.1
), we get (
A.2
).
∎

Lemma A.2
.

For
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
, it holds

\begin{split}\begin{cases}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{x_{j}-P_{j}}{2\pi|x-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\\[5.69054pt]
\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|x-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|x-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right),\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\partial H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=\frac{\partial H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\\[5.69054pt]
\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right).\end{cases}\end{split}

(A.3)

In particular,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}=&\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{\pi|y-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{4\pi G_{\Omega}(y,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\\
&+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right).\end{split}

(A.4)

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
A.1
,
for
j=1,2
, we define

\begin{split}b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y):=&\frac{\partial\big(H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)-H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{j}}-\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}-\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}+\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}.\end{split}

(A.5)

Then
\Delta_{x}b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=0
and for
x\in\partial\Omega
, it holds

b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}\right)
. Also for
x\in\partial B(P,\varepsilon)
,
we know that
b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=O\Big(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}\Big)
. Hence by the maximum principle, for
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
, we deduce that
b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)=O\Big(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}\Big)
. Thus, the second estimate of (
A.3
) holds. By similar computations, we can derive the first estimate in (
A.3
).
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Let
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
,
i,j=1,2
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\frac{1}{2\pi|x-P|^{2}}\Big(\delta_{ij}-\frac{2(x_{i}-P_{i})(x_{j}-P_{j})}{|x-P|^{2}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\ln|y-P|}{\ln\varepsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\frac{\ln|y-P|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|x-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right),

(A.6)

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}=&\frac{\partial^{2}H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}-\frac{2\pi}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\\
&+O\left(\frac{1}{dist\big\{x,\partial B(P,\varepsilon)\big\}}\Big(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\Big)\right),\end{split}

(A.7)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}+\frac{1}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}\Big(\delta_{ij}-\frac{2(y_{i}-P_{i})(y_{j}-P_{j})}{|y-P|^{2}}\Big)\Big(1-\frac{\ln|x-P|}{\ln\varepsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\frac{\ln|x-P|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right).

(A.8)

Proof.

First, for
i,j=1,2
, we define

\begin{split}\bar{b}_{\varepsilon,j,i}(x,y):=&\frac{\partial^{2}\big(H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)-H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}-\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}-\frac{1}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}\Big(\delta_{ij}-\frac{2(y_{i}-P_{i})(y_{j}-P_{j})}{|y-P|^{2}}\Big)\\
&+\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}.\end{split}

Then
\Delta_{x}\bar{b}_{\varepsilon,j,i}(x,y)=0
and for
x\in\partial\Omega
, it holds

\bar{b}_{\varepsilon,j,i}(x,y)=O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|}\right)
. Moreover for
x\in\partial B(P,\varepsilon)
,
we know that
\bar{b}_{\varepsilon,j,i}(x,y)=O\Big(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|^{2}}\Big)
. Hence for
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
, we deduce that
\bar{b}_{\varepsilon,j,i}(x,y)=O\Big(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|^{2}}\Big)
. Also, it holds

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}=&\frac{1}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}\Big(\delta_{ij}-\frac{2(y_{i}-P_{i})(y_{j}-P_{j})}{|y-P|^{2}}\Big)\frac{\ln|x-P|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\\
&+\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\frac{\ln|x-P|}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|y-P|^{2}\cdot|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big),\end{split}

and then (
A.3
) follows. Similarly we derive (
A.3
).

To prove (
A.7
), we first note that the function
b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)

defined in
(
A.5
) is harmonic in
x
. So we have (page 22 in
[
14
]
)

|\nabla_{x}b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C}{dist\big\{x,\partial B(P,\varepsilon)\big\}}|b_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)|
,
which, together with (
A.3
), gives (
A.7
).
∎

At the end of this subsection, we give the proof of a result on
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)
(see Lemma
3.2
).

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

Since
\Omega
is smooth, there exists
d_{0}>0
such that for any
x\in\Omega
with
dist\{x,\partial\Omega\}<d_{0}
, there exists a unique
x^{\prime}\in\partial\Omega
, satisfying
dist\{x,\partial\Omega\}=|x-x^{\prime}|
. By translation and rotation, we assume that
x=(0,d_{x})
,
x^{\prime}=0
, and there is a
C^{1}
function
\phi(y_{1})
such that
\phi(0)=0
,

\nabla\phi(0)=0
and

\partial\Omega\cap B(0,\delta)=\bigl\{y:\;y_{2}=\phi(y_{1})\bigr\}\cap B(0,\delta),~~\Omega\cap B(0,\delta)=\bigl\{y:\;y_{2}>\phi(y_{1})\bigr\}\cap B(0,\delta),

where
\delta>0
is a small constant.
Let
x^{\prime\prime}=(0,-d_{x})
be the reflection of
x
with respect to the boundary of
\Omega
. For
d_{0}
small enough,
x^{\prime\prime}\not\in\Omega
. The function

\ln\frac{1}{|y-x^{\prime\prime}|}

is harmonic in
\Omega
.
Since
\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}}
is a harmonic function in
\Omega
and on the boundary
\partial\Omega
, we have, for
i=1,2
,

\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{x_{i}-y_{i}}{|x-y|^{2}}.

We consider two functions, defined on
\Omega
, in the following way

f_{1}(y):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{y_{1}}{|x^{\prime\prime}-y|^{2}},~~~~~~f_{2}(y):=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{d_{x}+y_{2}}{|x^{\prime\prime}-y|^{2}}.

We can verify that

\Delta_{y}\Big(\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}}-f_{i}(y)\Big)=0,~~\mbox{for}~~y\in\Omega~~~\mbox{and}~~~i=1,2.

Also
for any
y\in\partial\Omega
, in view of
|y_{2}|=|\phi(y_{1})|=O(|y_{1}|^{2})
, it holds

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{1}}-f_{1}(y)=

\displaystyle\frac{d_{x}}{2\pi}\left(\frac{1}{|x-y|^{2}}-\frac{1}{|x^{\prime\prime}-y|^{2}}\right)=\frac{d_{x}}{2\pi}\left(\frac{1}{|y|^{2}+d_{x}^{2}-2d_{x}y_{2}}-\frac{1}{|y|^{2}+d_{x}^{2}+2d_{x}y_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{4d_{x}^{2}y_{2}}{(|y|^{2}+d_{x}^{2})^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{4d_{x}^{2}|y_{1}|^{2}}{(|y|^{2}+d_{x}^{2})^{2}}\right)=O\big(1\big),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{2}}-f_{2}(y)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\frac{y_{2}-d_{x}}{y_{1}^{2}+(y_{2}-d_{x})^{2}}-\frac{y_{2}+d_{x}}{y_{1}^{2}+(y_{2}+d_{x})^{2}}\right)=O\big(d_{x}\big),

here we use the fact that letting
f(x)=\frac{x}{1+x^{2}}
, then
|f^{\prime}(x)|\leq 3
.
Hence by the maximum principle, we get

\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{i}}=f_{i}(y)+O\big(1\big),~~~\mbox{for}~~~i=1,2,

uniformly in
y\in\Omega
as
d_{x}\to 0
, and we get that as
d_{x}\to 0
,

\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)}{\partial x_{1}}=2\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,x)}{\partial x_{1}}=O\big(1\big)~~~~\,\,\mbox{and}~~~~\,\,\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)}{\partial x_{2}}=2\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,x)}{\partial x_{2}}=-\frac{1}{2\pi d_{x}}+O\big(1\big).

This completes the proof of (
3.5
).
∎

A.2.
Estimates for Kirchhoff-Routh function

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

From (
2.1
), (
A.1
) and (
A.2
), we get

\begin{split}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=&\mathcal{KR}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\left(\Lambda_{1}^{2}\ln\frac{|x-P|^{2}}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}^{2}\ln\frac{|y-P|^{2}}{\varepsilon}+2\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|x-P|\cdot|y-P|}{\varepsilon}\right)\\
&+\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)-\frac{2\pi(\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,P)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(y,P))^{2}}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}-\frac{\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}}{\pi}\ln|x-y|+O\left(\frac{1}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).\end{split}

Also we can compute

\begin{split}\big(\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,P)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(y,P)\big)^{2}=\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\Big[\Lambda_{1}\ln|x-P|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y-P|+2\pi\big(\Lambda_{1}H_{\Omega}(x,P)+\Lambda_{2}H_{\Omega}(y,P)\big)\Big]^{2}.\end{split}

Hence collecting the above computations, we deduce (
2.2
).
∎

Now we give the fundamental estimate of
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

From (
A.3
) and (
A.4
), we find that

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=&\Lambda^{2}_{2}\Bigg[\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{\pi|y-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{4\pi G_{\Omega}(y,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\Bigg]-2\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial S(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\\
&+2\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\Bigg[\frac{\partial H_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{2\pi G_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\Bigg]\\
&+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right)\\
=&\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+2\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial S(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})(y_{j}-P_{j})}{\pi|y-P|^{2}}\\
&-\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\times\frac{4\pi\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,P)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(y,P)\big)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon}{|\ln\varepsilon|\cdot|y-P|}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}+\varepsilon\right).\end{split}

(A.9)

Next, we know

G_{\Omega}(x,P)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln|x-P|-H_{\Omega}(P,x)\,\,\mbox{and}\,\,\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{y_{j}-P_{j}}{2\pi|y-P|^{2}}-\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}.

Then it holds

\begin{split}&\frac{4\pi\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,P)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(y,P)\big)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(P,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\\
&=\frac{\Lambda_{2}(\Lambda_{1}\ln|x-P|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y-P|)y_{j}}{\pi(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P))|y-P|^{2}}+O\Big(\frac{1}{|y-P|\cdot|\ln\varepsilon|}+\frac{|\ln|x-P||}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\Big).\end{split}

(A.10)

Combining the above computations, we obtain

\begin{split}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=&\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{(B(P,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+2\Lambda_{1}\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial S(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\\
&-\frac{\Lambda_{2}y_{j}(\Lambda_{1}\ln\frac{|x-P|}{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{2}\ln\frac{|y-P|}{\varepsilon})}{\pi|y-P|^{2}(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P))}+O\left(\frac{1}{|y-P|\cdot|\ln\varepsilon|}+\big|\frac{\ln|x-P|}{\ln\varepsilon}\big|+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{|y-P|^{2}}\right).\end{split}

This proves the second identity in (
3.11
). The first identity in (
3.11
) can be proved in a similar manner. Finally (
3.12
) can be deduced by (
A.3
), (
A.7
) and (
A.3
) as in the previous case.

It remains to prove (
3.13
).
In fact, for
P=0
and
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, we can compute (
A.10
) more precisely as follows

\begin{split}&\frac{4\pi\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(y,0)\big)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\frac{\partial G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\\
=&\frac{\Lambda_{2}\big(\Lambda_{1}\ln|x|+\Lambda_{2}\ln|y|+2\pi(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)\big)y_{j}}{\pi(\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0))|y|^{2}}+\underbrace{O\left(\frac{|x|+|y|}{|y|\cdot|\ln\varepsilon|}+\frac{|\ln x|}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right)}_{=O(1)}.\end{split}

(A.11)

Inserting (
A.11
) into (
A.9
) with
P=0
, we get the second equation in (
3.13
). As before the first equation can de deduced in a very similar way. This completes the proof.
∎

Before we end this section, we discuss the expansions for
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
if
x
and
y
are close to
P
.
From now we assume that
P=0
and, from Theorem
1.13
(2), if
(x_{\varepsilon},y_{\varepsilon})
is a type III critical point of
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
, we have
|x_{\varepsilon}|,|y_{\varepsilon}|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
.
Now we expand
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
on
\big\{(x_{,}y)\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}\times\Omega_{\varepsilon};|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}\big\}
.

Lemma A.4
.

For any
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}:=\Omega\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
with
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, we have, for
j=1,2
,

\frac{\partial H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=\frac{\partial H_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\frac{G_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{2\pi}+\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{y_{j}}{2\pi|y|^{2}}\left[\frac{G_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{2\pi}+\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+1\right]+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),

and

\frac{\partial H_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial H_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{2\pi}+\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+\frac{x_{j}}{2\pi|x|^{2}}\left[\frac{G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\frac{\ln\varepsilon}{2\pi}+\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}+1\right]+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right).

Proof.

Since
P=0
and
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, the results follow
directly from (
A.3
).
∎

Using the above expansions we derive the following estimates on
\nabla\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)
.

Proposition A.5
.

For any
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}:=\Omega\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
with
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, it holds for
j=1,2
,

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{B_{\varepsilon}^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\\[14.22636pt]
\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{B_{\varepsilon}^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{cases}

(A.12)

where

\begin{split}\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y):=&\Lambda_{1}\left[\Big(\frac{x_{j}}{|x|^{2}}+2\pi\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,0)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right.\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big(\Lambda_{1}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x)}{\partial x_{j}}+2\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)+\frac{(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})x_{j}}{\pi|x|^{2}}\right],\end{split}

(A.13)

\begin{split}\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y):=&\Lambda_{2}\left[\Big(\frac{y_{j}}{|y|^{2}}+2\pi\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big)\frac{\Lambda_{1}G_{\Omega}(x,0)+\Lambda_{2}G_{\Omega}(0,y)}{\ln\varepsilon+2\pi\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(0)}\right.\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+\Big(2\Lambda_{1}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+\Lambda_{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big)+\frac{(\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2})y_{j}}{\pi|y|^{2}}\right].\end{split}

(A.14)

Proof.

For any
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}
with
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
and
j=1,2
, the second estimate of (
A.12
) holds from (
A.9
) directly.
Similarly we derive the first estimate of (
A.12
).
∎

Lemma A.6
.

For any
x,y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}:=\Omega\backslash B(0,\varepsilon)
with
|x|,|y|\sim\varepsilon^{\beta}
, we have, for
j=1,2
,

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}+\frac{\partial\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}\partial y_{i}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}\partial y_{i}}+\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,i}(x,y)}{\partial y_{j}}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\\[8.53581pt]
\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{i}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{(B(0,\varepsilon))^{c}}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{i}}+\frac{\partial\Psi_{\varepsilon,i}(x,y)}{\partial x_{j}}+O\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{1-2\beta}}{|\ln\varepsilon|}\right),\end{cases}

where
\Psi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)
and
\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}(x,y)

are the functions in (
A.13
) and (
A.14
).

Proof.

Using Lemma
A.3
, we can prove this lemma in a similar way
as in Proposition
A.5
.
∎

Appendix B
Examples

In this section, we provide some examples of domains that satisfy the assumptions of our main results.

1. A disk with punctured holes
.

For any fixed
y_{0}\in B(0,1)
with
|y_{0}|
closing to
1
, let
\Omega=B(0,1)\backslash B(y_{0},\delta)
, where
\delta
is small. Then, from Theorem
1.7
,
\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)

has a type II critical point

(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})
,
satisfying
x_{\delta}\to x_{0}
(
x_{0}\neq 0
) and
y_{\delta}\to y_{0}
as
\delta\to 0
. Hence

\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}}=0
. We also have
that

|x_{0}|
closes to
0
since
|y_{0}|
closes to
1
. We have following result.

Proposition B.1
.

Let
\Omega=B(0,1)\backslash B(y_{0},\delta)

and
(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})
be as above. Then following results hold.

(1)

The matrix
\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}
is invertible and the matrix

\textbf{M}_{0}=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}-\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\left(\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}

has two different positive eigenvalues.

(2)

The matrix

\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}:=\left[\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},x_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}-3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta})}{\partial y_{j}}\right]_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}

has two different eigenvalues.

Proof.

(1) First, by (
5.33
)(Swapping the order of
x
and
y
) we have

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+o_{\delta}\left(1\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}+o\left(\frac{1}{|y_{\delta}-y_{0}|}\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{\Lambda^{2}_{2}}{\pi}\left[\frac{\delta_{ij}}{|y_{\delta}-y_{0}|^{2}}-\frac{2(y_{\delta,i}-y_{0,i})(y_{\delta,j}-y_{0,j})}{|y_{\delta}-y_{0}|^{4}}\right]+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}+o_{\delta}\left(1\right).\end{cases}

(B.1)

Let us compute the terms involving
\nabla^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}
in the right hand side of (
B.1
).
From (
5.18
) and

\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}}=0
, we find

x_{0}\parallel y_{0}
. Hence by direct computations, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}=&\frac{\Lambda_{1}\lambda_{1}}{\pi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{2\Lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}}{\pi}\frac{x_{0,i}x_{0,j}}{|x_{0}|^{2}},\end{split}

with

\begin{split}\lambda_{1}:=\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{1-|x_{0}|^{2}}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}|y_{0}|^{2}}{(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{2}}\right),~~\lambda_{2}:=\left(\frac{\Lambda_{1}|x_{0}|^{2}}{(1-|x_{0}|^{2})^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}|y_{0}|^{2}}{(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Similarly, by direct calculations, we get

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}=O(1)~~~\mbox{and}~~~\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{(x,y)=(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}=O(1).\end{split}

This shows that

\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}
is invertible, since its two eigenvalues are

\mu_{\delta,1}=\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi|y_{\delta}-y_{0}|^{2}}\Big(1+o(1)\Big),~~~~\mu_{\delta,2}=-\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2}}{\pi|y_{\delta}-y_{0}|^{2}}\Big(1+o(1)\Big).

The above computations also yield

\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial x_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\left(\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{KR}_{\Omega}(x_{\delta},y_{\delta})}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}o_{\delta}(1)&o_{\delta}(1)\\[5.69054pt]
o_{\delta}(1)&o_{\delta}(1)\\
\end{array}\right).

Hence the eigenvalues of

\bf{M}_{0}
are given by

\lambda_{\delta,1}=\frac{\Lambda_{1}\lambda_{1}}{\pi}+o_{\delta}(1),~~~\lambda_{\delta,2}=\frac{\Lambda_{1}(\lambda_{1}+2\lambda_{2})}{\pi}+o_{\delta}(1).

Here we point out that if
y_{0}
closes to
\partial B(0,1)
, by Theorem
1.7
, we know that
|x_{0}|
closes to
0
and

\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}}-\frac{\Lambda_{2}|y_{0}|^{2}}{(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{2}}=\frac{(1-|y_{0}|^{2})(1+|y_{0}|^{2}-2|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|)}{(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{2}}~~\mbox{closes to }~0.

Note that

|x_{0}|
closes to
0
since
|y_{0}|
closes to
1
. Thus
\lambda_{1}
closes to
\Lambda_{1}

and

\lambda_{2}
closes to
0
.
Hence
\lambda_{\delta,1}>0~~~\mbox{and}~~~\lambda_{\delta,2}>0
if
|y_{0}|
is close to 1.

To prove that
\lambda_{\delta,1}\neq\lambda_{\delta,2}
. we just need to prove
\lambda_{2}\neq 0
. Now from

\frac{\partial\mathcal{KR}_{B(0,1)}(x_{0},y_{0})}{\partial x_{i}}=0
for
i=1,2
, we have

\begin{split}\frac{\Lambda_{1}}{1-|x_{0}|^{2}}=\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}}+\frac{\Lambda_{2}|y_{0}|}{(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)|x_{0}|}.\end{split}

Putting this into the definition of
\lambda_{2}
, we have

\lambda_{2}=\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{2}(1-|x_{0}|^{2})(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{2}}\Big((|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{3}+(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{3}|y_{0}|\Big).

Now we can compute

(|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|-1)^{3}+(|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{3}|y_{0}|=\underbrace{\Big((|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{3}(|y_{0}|-1)\Big)}_{<0}+\underbrace{\Big((|y_{0}|-|x_{0}|)^{3}-(1-|x_{0}|\cdot|y_{0}|)^{3}\Big)}_{<0}<0.

Hence
\textbf{M}_{0}
has two different positive eigenvalues.

(2) Since
|x_{\delta}-y_{0}|\geq C_{0}>0
with
C_{0}
independent of
\delta
, combining the computations in Remark
7.24
, for
i,j=1,2
, we obtain

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=x_{\delta}}=\frac{\partial^{2}H_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=x_{\delta}}+o_{\delta}(1)=\frac{|x_{0}|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|x_{0}|^{2})^{2}}\left(\delta_{ij}-\frac{2x_{0,i}x_{0,j}}{|x_{0}|^{2}}\right)+o_{\delta}(1),

and

\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{i}}\Big|_{y=x_{\delta}}=\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{B(0,1)}(y)}{\partial y_{i}}\Big|_{y=x_{\delta}}+o_{\delta}(1)=-\frac{x_{0,i}}{\pi(1-|x_{0}|^{2})}+o_{\delta}(1).

Now we have

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=x_{\delta}}-3\pi\Big[\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(y)}{\partial y_{j}}\Big]\Big|_{y=x_{\delta}}=\frac{|x_{0}|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|x_{0}|^{2})^{2}}\left(\delta_{ij}-\frac{8x_{0,i}x_{0,j}}{|x_{0}|^{2}}\right)+o_{\delta}(1).

Hence we find that the two eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}
are

\frac{|x_{0}|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|x_{0}|^{2})^{2}}+o_{\delta}(1)
and
-\frac{7|x_{0}|^{2}}{2\pi(1-|x_{0}|^{2})^{2}}+o_{\delta}(1)
.

∎

2. A result in a general domain
.

Proposition B.2
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain. If
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}
and
dist\{P,\partial\Omega\}
is small, then
the matrix

{\widetilde{\bf{M}}}:=\left(\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(P,P)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}-3\pi\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}{\partial x_{j}}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}

has two different eigenvalues.

Proof.

Using the computations in Lemma
5.6
, we have that
H_{\Omega}(dz,P)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{1}{|z+e_{2}|}-\frac{\ln d}{2\pi}+o(1)
, where
d:=dist\{P,\partial\Omega\}
. Hence

\begin{cases}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{1}}=\frac{1}{d}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(dz,P)}{\partial z_{1}}=\frac{1}{d}\left(-\frac{z_{1}}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{2}}+o(1)\right),\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{2}}=\frac{1}{d}\frac{\partial H_{\Omega}(dz,P)}{\partial z_{2}}=\frac{1}{d}\left(-\frac{z_{2}+1}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{2}}+o(1)\right).\end{cases}

And then it holds

\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}{\partial x_{1}}=o\Big(\frac{1}{d}\Big),~~~~~~\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}(P)}{\partial x_{2}}=\frac{1}{d}\left(-\frac{1}{\pi}+o(1)\right).

Furthermore,

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{1}^{2}}\Big|_{x=P}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{2}}+\frac{2z_{1}^{2}}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{4}}+o(1)\right)\Big|_{z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(0,1)}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{4\pi}+o(1)\right),

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{1}\partial x_{2}}\Big|_{x=P}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(\frac{2z_{1}(z_{2}+1)}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{4}}+o(1)\right)\Big|_{z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(0,1)}=o\left(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right),

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega}(x,P)}{\partial x_{2}^{2}}\Big|_{x=P}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{2}}+\frac{2(z_{2}+1)^{2}}{\pi|z+e_{2}|^{4}}+o(1)\right)\Big|_{z=(z_{1},z_{2})=(0,1)}=\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{4\pi}+o(1)\right).

Hence we obtain

{\widetilde{\bf{M}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{4\pi}+o(1)\right)&o\left(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)\\[5.69054pt]
o\left(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\right)&\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{4\pi}-\frac{3}{\pi}+o(1)\right)\\
\end{array}\right).

And then the two eigenvalues of
\widetilde{\mathbf{M}}
are

\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{1}{4\pi}+o(1)\right)
and
\frac{1}{d^{2}}\left(-\frac{11}{4\pi}+o(1)\right)
.
∎

3. A result in an ellipse
.

Lemma B.3
.

Let

\Omega_{\delta}=\Big\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\;x_{1}^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{1}\delta\big)^{2}+x_{2}^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{2}\delta\big)^{2}<1,\ \delta>0,\ \alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\geq 0\Big\}.

Then Robin function
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega_{\delta}}(x)
has a unique critical point
P=0
and is even with respect to
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
. Moreover, for
\delta
small, it holds

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}=-\frac{\delta}{2\pi}\left[\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{2}\alpha_{m}\Big)\right]\delta_{ij}+O\left(\delta^{2}\right),

(B.2)

and

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}=\left[-\frac{1}{2\pi}+\frac{\delta}{2\pi}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha_{i}\Big)\right]\delta_{ij}+O\!\left(\delta^{2}\right).

(B.3)

Proof.

First, since
\Omega_{\delta}
is symmetric with respect to both
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
, the Robin function
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega_{\delta}}
is even in
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
. Moreover, since
\Omega_{\delta}
is convex, classical results (see
[
5
,
10
]
) imply that
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega_{\delta}}
has a unique critical point, namely
P=0
.

Now from the computations of the Robin function in Theorem 6.1 of
[
15
]
, we obtain that for any
x,y\in\Omega_{\delta}
,

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\delta}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}{H}_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}-\frac{\delta}{2\pi}\frac{\partial^{2}\big((|y|^{2}-1)v(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}+O\big(\delta^{2}+|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\big)\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\delta}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}=\frac{\partial^{2}{H}_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}-\frac{\delta}{2\pi}\frac{\partial^{2}\big((|y|^{2}-1)v(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}+O\big(\delta^{2}+|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\big),\end{split}

where

v(x,y)=-\frac{1}{2}(|x|^{2}-1)+\sum^{2}_{i=1}\alpha_{i}x_{i}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\langle x,y\rangle+\sum^{2}_{i=1}\alpha_{i}x_{i}y_{i}+\frac{|y|^{2}}{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{8}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\alpha_{i}\Big)+\frac{1}{16}\sum^{2}_{i=1}\Big(1+4\alpha_{i}\Big)y_{i}^{2}.

On the other hand, direct computations yield

\begin{split}\frac{\partial^{2}\big((|y|^{2}-1)v(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=0}=-\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}+\alpha_{i}\Big)\delta_{ij},~~~\,\,\frac{\partial^{2}\big((|y|^{2}-1)v(x,y)\big)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=0}=\left[\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{2}\alpha_{m}\Big)\right]\delta_{ij}.\end{split}

Also we compute

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=0}=\left(\frac{2x_{j}y_{i}-\delta_{ij}}{2\pi\big||x|y-\frac{x}{|x|}\big|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{(|x|^{2}y_{i}-x_{i})(|y|^{2}x_{j}-y_{j})}{\big||x|y-\frac{x}{|x|}\big|^{4}}\right)\Big|_{x=y=0}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\delta_{ij},

and

\frac{\partial^{2}H_{B(0,1)}(x,y)}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{j}}\Big|_{x=y=0}=\left[\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{|x|^{2}}{\big||x|y-\frac{x}{|x|}\big|^{2}}\delta_{ij}-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{(|x|^{2}y_{i}-x_{i})(|x|^{2}y_{j}-x_{j})}{\big||x|y-\frac{x}{|x|}\big|^{4}}\right]\Big|_{x=y=0}=0.

Hence (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) hold by above computations.
∎

Proposition B.4
.

Let
\Omega_{\delta}=\Big\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},x_{1}^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{1}\delta\big)^{2}+x_{2}^{2}\big(1+\alpha_{2}\delta\big)^{2}<1,~~\delta>0,~~\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\geq 0,\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}\Big\}
, then
{\overline{\bf{M}}}:=\left[(\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\right]_{1\leq i,j\leq 2}
has two different eigenvalues for
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
.

Proof.

Using (
B.2
) and (
B.3
), we have

\begin{split}&(\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}+(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}H_{\Omega_{\delta}}(0,0)}{\partial y_{i}\partial x_{j}}\\
=&\left\{-\frac{(\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\delta}{2\pi}\left[\frac{3}{2}-2\alpha_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\displaystyle\sum^{2}_{m=1}\alpha_{m}\Big)\right]+(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}\left[-\frac{1}{2\pi}+\frac{\delta}{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\alpha_{i}\Big)\right]\right\}\delta_{ij}+O\Big(\delta^{2}\Big)\\
=&\left[-\frac{(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}}{2\pi}+\frac{\delta}{4\pi}\Big((\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\displaystyle\sum^{2}_{m=1}\alpha_{m}+6(\tau^{4}+1)\alpha_{i}-(2\tau^{4}+5\tau^{2}+2)\Big)\right]\delta_{ij}+O\Big(\delta^{2}\Big).\end{split}

Hence, letting
\mu_{i}
for
i=1,2
be the eigenvalues of
\overline{\bf{M}}
, we find

\mu_{i}=\left[-\frac{(\tau^{2}-1)^{2}}{2\pi}+\frac{\delta}{4\pi}\Big((\tau^{4}+\tau^{2}+1)\displaystyle\sum^{2}_{m=1}\alpha_{m}+6(\tau^{4}+1)\alpha_{i}-(2\tau^{4}+5\tau^{2}+2)\Big)\right]+O\Big(\delta^{2}\Big),~~\mbox{for}~~i=1,2.

Thus, if
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
, the two eigenvalues of
\overline{\bf{M}}
are different for
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}]
.
∎
